## Resume checkpoint — 2026-10-03 (Pass 1 in progress)

This continuation has not completed a full audit pass. Do not mark modules or exit conditions complete from these partial results.

**Freshness correction:** An earlier route crawl was contaminated by a Vite source reload. A clean 97/97 rerun later passed after frontend edits stopped; its current-pass evidence is recorded below. Older broad-suite results remain historical and do not replace current-pass checks.

### Verified

- Real-app route crawl: 97/97 Playwright tests; 16 internal roles × 142 routes = 2,272 visits. All role artifacts report zero blank pages, login bounces, forbidden screens, console errors, or failed API responses. Artifacts: `spa/e2e-real/results/crawl-*.json`. This proves route rendering only, not action permissions.
- Leave request chain: `spa/e2e-real/leave-self-service-chain.spec.ts` passes 1/1 (21.2 s) against the isolated browser DB, with employee filing, department-head approval, HR approval, and employee cancellation through the real UI. The test reads request, queue, audit, notification, balance, and attendance state after each transition. No captured console error or failed API response.
- Leave regression: the first real UI run exposed that request numbers were inert and no owner detail route existed. The list now links to the shared detail page; the owner route uses `/leaves/balances/me`, shows a return link to the owner list, and resolves approval-notification URLs. `tsc --noEmit`, targeted ESLint, `LeaveDetailPage` Vitest (1/1), `impeccable detect` (no findings), and the real chain pass. Three audit-created requests in the browser test DB are all `cancelled`; the green run also verified balance and attendance restoration.
- API suites after the shared approval SoD guard: Leave + HR 341 tests / 1,586 assertions; Approvals 32 / 82; Accounting 297 / 4,836 on a fresh full-module rerun; Loans 53 / 253; Assets 59 / 355; Return Management 137 / 832. Returns reported one deprecated doc-comment data provider; it is now a native `DataProvider` attribute, and the affected file passes 10 tests / 29 assertions with no PHPUnit deprecation. Inventory passed 366 tests / 1,566 assertions on a fresh full-module rerun with no PHPUnit deprecations; its peak reported usage was 127 MB under the existing 128 MB PHP default. Four earlier provider warnings are gone. No memory setting changed.
- Purchasing passed all nine isolated shards: 381 tests / 1,550 assertions. The first unsharded directory run exceeded PHP's existing 128 MB default. Shard i exposed a stale test expectation: seeded department heads lack `purchasing.pr.approve`, so the pending-count route correctly returns 403. The regression now asserts that denial and keeps the zero-count assertion for a department-head user with an explicit per-user grant but no workflow step. No memory limit was changed.
- Fresh SPA verification: Vitest 72 files / 417 tests, TypeScript typecheck, full ESLint, and production build pass. Build reports one chunking warning because `NotFound.tsx` is both statically and dynamically imported; chunk splitting is ineffective for that module, with runtime impact unverified. API route audit matched 974 request shapes / 1,605 Laravel method/routes / 14 declared exceptions; RBAC static audit reports 267 catalog entries, 262 static references, zero unseeded references and five seeded permissions without scanned references; token audit passed 855 files. Fresh real-browser role-permission sweep passed 866 checks across all 16 roles with zero failures; mobile navigation was included. Cross-role dashboard panel gates passed 5/5. Real Accounting maker-checker/prebill/reversal E2E passed 5/5; COA actions/audit/permissions passed 4/4; invoice recovery passed 1/1 via Finance UI. Auth sign-in passed 32/32 for 16 roles; deactivation and security-header/CORS E2E passed 3/3. Fresh Inventory browser suite passed 18/18 across adjustment approval, transfer SoD, item/product catalogs, UOM editing/lookups, stock-count completion and variance SoD. Intentional negative probes returned 404/403/422 with unchanged state; no 5xx or PHP fatal appeared in captured logs. One Nginx 499 was a products-list GET cancelled as the E2E page closed after navigation; no response body or side effect, not reproduced as an application error. Procurement PR→PO→GRN→QC→draft-bill now has a real-browser acceptance run, including failed automation-actor recovery; Inventory MRB and material-issue UI chains remain unverified.
- Fresh Leave + HR + Approvals rerun passed 374 tests / 1,684 assertions, peaking at 127 MB under PHP's existing 128 MB limit. The first run executed all 374 cases but exited 255 when PHPUnit exhausted memory writing its optional result cache; rerunning with `--do-not-cache-result` passed. No memory setting changed. The run emitted only classified failure-path logs: bulk-approval SQL detail sanitization, recruitment mail failure with an in-app alert, deactivated year-end actor handoff, a deliberately missing `employee_property` table, and onboarding reminder no-audience/delivery-failure retry paths.
- Fresh year-end leave UI test passed 1/1; the API regression proves an inactive staged actor leaves balances untouched and hands off durably, then an authorized replacement creates one fresh outbox attempt, carries the expected balance disposition, updates the processed marker, and can safely repeat the request. `YearEndLeaveDurableHandoffTest` passed inside the full API run. Dashboard chart regressions passed: fixed-size trend, scorecard sparkline and OEE gauge render without Recharts size warnings. Both fixes have focused commits: `ba080a80` and `21ed4335`.
- Fresh static checks: API route audit matched 974 SPA request shapes to 1,605 Laravel method/routes with 14 declared exceptions; RBAC audit found no missing grants and the same five seeded permissions without scanned runtime references; token scan passed across 855 files; `npm audit --omit=dev` found zero vulnerabilities; `git diff --check` passed.

### Fresh checks — 2026-10-03 continuation

- SPA Vitest passed 72 files / 417 tests. TypeScript typecheck, full ESLint, API route audit (974 request shapes / 1,605 Laravel routes / 14 declared exceptions), RBAC static audit (267 catalog entries / 262 references / 5 unreferenced grants), token scan (856 files), `npm audit --omit=dev` (0 vulnerabilities), `composer audit` (0 advisories, 0 abandoned packages), and production build passed. Build repeats the existing Vite warning: `NotFound.tsx` is statically imported by `PermissionGuard.tsx` and dynamically imported by `App.tsx`; chunk-splitting impact remains unverified.
- Clean real-app route crawl passed 97/97 in 25.0 minutes from `spa/` with `E2E_BASE_URL=http://localhost:18080`, both `e2e-real/crawl.spec.ts` and `e2e-real/crawl-roles.spec.ts`, four workers. All 16 internal roles visited 142 routes each (2,272 total); artifact audit found zero blank pages, forbidden screens, login bounces, failed API requests, or console errors. No `spa/src` files changed during the run. This proves route rendering only, not action permissions or complete page behavior.
- Server-log review for that run found 19,367 HTTP 200, 96 HTTP 204, 92 WebSocket 101, and 1 HTTP 304, with no application-generated 4xx/5xx responses. Thirteen Nginx 499 client-abort records were GET lookups for PO lists, products, and customers whose pages the route crawler left after its fixed 2.5-second observation window; they caused no write and are harness-driven cancellations. PHP-FPM emitted one `pm.max_children=5` saturation warning under four browser workers. No server error followed; production pool sizing and realistic-load latency remain UNVERIFIED.
- Corrected dynamic-route audit is running with `BASE_URL=http://localhost:18080`; the first invocation used the script's default `http://localhost` and failed with `ERR_SSL_PROTOCOL_ERROR` before any probe, so it is invalid harness evidence.
- Attempted concurrent Accounting, Assets, and Loans PHP suites against separate test databases while also starting a nonexistent `tests/Feature/Returns` path. PostgreSQL returned SQLSTATE `53200` / `out of shared memory` during concurrent `DROP TABLE` schema resets; Assets ended with 59 errors / 0 assertions and Loans with 43 errors / 67 assertions before application tests could run. Accounting was interrupted after the same migration contention; the Returns path ran no tests. Classify all as harness contention and UNVERIFIED, then rerun database-reset suites serially. PHP `memory_limit` remained the existing `128M`; no memory override was used.
- Source sweep found a real no-op pagination callback in the Leave Types manager (`spa/src/pages/leaves/types.tsx`). Two browser red runs reproduced it against more than 100 test-only leave types: the UI ignored the page query and then retained page 2 after switching to Archived. Page state and archive-scope reset are now wired to the API; fresh real-browser E2E passed 1/1 and the manager Vitest passed 2/2. Recruitment's empty filter callbacks are paired with `filters={}` (no filter controls), so no dead filter control was observed there.

### Defects fixed in this continuation

- **SoD, critical:** `ApprovalService` now rejects one actor deciding twice in the same approval attempt, for both approve and reject. Regression test proves second decision is 403 and preserves request status, approvals, rejection reason, balance, and attendance. Leave/HR and approval suites pass after the guard. No migration.
- **Self-service leave dead end, functional:** employee request details and approval notification links now open the existing detail page. The employee can see their own balance and return to their leave list; API authorization remains owner-scoped. Guarded by the real multi-role chain test.
- **Future-dated invoice cancellation, high:** cancelling a posted prebill dated after today used `now()` as its reversal date; the journal server rejected it before any writes. API regression was observed red (422) then green (1 test / 28 assertions) after the service chooses the later of today and the source journal date. The full real-browser Accounting chain passed 5/5, including reversal and trial-balance assertions. A previously blocked invoice was also cancelled through Finance UI; its source journal was reversed and reversal date matched source date. No migration.
- **Year-end leave actor recovery, high:** red API reproduction showed that an active HR/System Admin retry after the queued actor was deactivated reused the failed outbox payload and could not complete the year-end disposition. `YearEndLeaveProcessingService` now uses the root outbox row as a per-year/scope lock and stages a new idempotent attempt only after the exact `year_end_automation_actor_unavailable` handoff. The test proves unchanged state before recovery, replacement actor attribution, expected balance/disposition/processed-marker effects, one effect on repeat, and resolution of the original handoff. Real HR UI enqueue/listener E2E passed 1/1. Commit `ba080a80`; no migration.
- **Dashboard fixed-size chart warning, low:** KPI trends, scorecard sparklines, and the OEE gauge used `ResponsiveContainer` around charts with fixed dimensions. Three tests reproduced the warning before the fix; they now render directly at their declared dimensions and assert the warning is absent. Commit `21ed4335`.

- The first route crawl completed 90/90 chunks against 15 non-admin roles × 142 static non-portal routes (2,130 visits), but is invalid permission evidence: it used four workers and full-page loads, causing 554 API 429s, 42 API 403s, 76 login bounces, and 494 console errors (452 429 messages, 42 403 messages). Five pages rendered forbidden text: Finance `/accounting/coa`, `/accounting/coa/create`, `/accounting/invoices/create`, `/accounting/journal-entries/create`; Vice President `/accounting/coa`. The final isolated rerun passed 91/91 Playwright tests (including the forbidden-text detector regression) and visited all 2,130 route/role pairs: 15 roles × 142 routes. Each role artifact has exactly 142 rows and zero forbidden flags, blank pages, login bounces, failed API responses, or console errors. API container logs for 2026-10-02 06:36:10–07:02:46 UTC contain 1,842 HTTP 200 and 92 HTTP 204 responses, with no other statuses. Nginx logs contain 68 warnings, all for large Vite JavaScript responses buffered to temporary files, and no error/critical entries. Finance/VP forbidden flags in the earlier report were false positives: the matcher found account code 4030; the word-bounded detector's regression failed before and passed after the change. Raw route results: /tmp/kwatog-route-crawl-pass1-fixed-20261002/; raw server logs: /tmp/kwatog-route-crawl-pass1-api.log and /tmp/kwatog-route-crawl-pass1-nginx.log. System Admin's crawl also passed 6/6 chunks across 142 routes with zero blank pages, login bounces, failed APIs, or console errors. Its API logs show 292 HTTP 200 and 6 HTTP 204 responses with no other statuses; Nginx logs show 6 temporary-buffer warnings and no error/critical entries. Admin artifacts are in /tmp/kwatog-route-crawl-pass1-fixed-20261002/crawl-admin.json and raw logs are /tmp/kwatog-admin-crawl-pass1-api.log and /tmp/kwatog-admin-crawl-pass1-nginx.log. The initial form-lookup, notification-preference, and accounting text findings were reproduced and fixed or explained below; none appeared in the final route crawl. This is route-render evidence only; role/action permission cells still need direct API testing. Previous raw reports remain under /tmp/kwatog-audit-results-20261002/.
- The browser role-permission audit initially could not sign in because the freshly created isolated DB had not yet been seeded; after seeding, all 16 internal roles passed 606 checks with zero failures. Checks covered declared sidebar expectations, every visible sidebar route, and five explicit permission surfaces. This is UI evidence only: direct API checks for every permission-matrix cell, record-level IDOR, and action-level authorization remain unverified. The one-role admin run passed 90 checks; the full pass followed after seeding.
- Security finding: the two demo seeders containing fixed `password` credentials ran in a production app environment when invoked directly; `DemoAccountSeeder` wrote 18 internal users and `GoldenPathDemoSeeder` wrote a supplier portal user. Reproduced in rollback-only tests on `ogami_test_security_audit_20261002`; no production database was used. Added a production boot rejection when `SEED_DEMO_DATA=true` and a shared production guard at both credential-seeder entry points. Red→green: `ProductionAssertionsTest` 13/13 (26 assertions), `DemoAccountSeederTest` 4/4 (16 assertions); both internal and portal tables remain empty on rejection. Commit `107cff44`, no migration.
- Reverb origin finding: baseline config used `allowed_origins=['*']`. In the isolated test stack, setting that baseline reproduced `https://evil.example` receiving `pusher:connection_established`. Fix parses configured URL entries to the hostname Reverb actually compares, defaults from `REVERB_ALLOWED_ORIGINS`, then `FRONTEND_URL`, then `APP_URL`, and blocks empty or wildcard production allowlists (including `*.domain`). A WebSocket HTTP 101 alone is not acceptance: Reverb sends a Pusher frame after upgrade. After the fix, evil origin receives `pusher:error` / `Origin not allowed`; `http://localhost:18080` receives `pusher:connection_established`. The real SPA then connected and received `pusher_internal:subscription_succeeded` with 0 WebSocket errors and 0 API failures. Test-first wildcard assertion failed before the guard; `ProductionAssertionsTest` passes 17/17 (35 assertions), Pint and PHP lint pass. Commit `210813e3`; no migration.

### Current-run decision question

1. **Legacy permission slugs:** Scenario: an admin assigns only `crm.customers.manage`, `crm.so.create`, `quality.inspections.create`, or `quality.inspections.edit` to a custom role. Why unresolved: these slugs remain in the seeded permission catalog but have no runtime references; current routes use other slugs, and code does not define whether these are aliases or obsolete. Options: remove/migrate the slugs; map each legacy slug narrowly to its corresponding action; retain them as inert entries. Recommendation: confirm intended custom-role compatibility, then map only if backward compatibility is required; never expand create/edit into manage. Interim guard: current server checks exact active slugs and returns 403; no permission is silently broadened.
2. **Editing source-linked invoices:** Scenario: a delivery-generated invoice needs a correction while still draft. Why unresolved: invoice lines derive from confirmed delivery records, but the system does not define whether Finance may amend the invoice or must correct the source. Options: edit invoice lines and revalidate delivery totals; correct the delivery and regenerate the draft; prohibit edits and replace via credit note/reissue. Recommendation: correct the source before finalization, then use credit note/reissue after finalization. Interim guard: only manual prebill drafts are editable; source-linked standard drafts receive 422 and have no edit control.
3. **Invoice and prebill effective dates:** Scenario: Finance creates a future-dated or backdated invoice and posts it. Why unresolved: the roadmap says invoice timing policy still needs definition; no document sets a date window. Options: keep any valid date whose accounting period is not closed; restrict posting to the current/open period with explicit exceptions; require a separate reason and approval for dates outside the current period. Recommendation: keep the existing open-period gate until Finance sets the supported date window. Interim guard: date must parse, due date must not precede invoice date, and explicitly closed periods return 422; no extra date restriction was added. Observed: the real UI prebill chain posted a future-dated invoice; a Finance maker/checker/poster API walk posted a `2026-06-15` invoice and cancellation reversed its journal in the isolated DB.
4. **Manual prebill policy:** Scenario: Finance needs an invoice before confirmed delivery. Why unresolved: the roadmap says an approved prebill/pro-forma lifecycle should exist only if the business requires it, and does not say whether it may post AR and revenue before delivery. Options: keep the current reasoned three-actor posting flow; convert it to a non-posting pro-forma that converts after delivery; require confirmed delivery before any invoice. Recommendation: use a non-posting pro-forma if prebilling is required. Interim guard: existing prebill creation requires a reason, approval, and a different poster; standard invoices without a confirmed delivery are rejected. Existing behavior remains pending policy.

A failing real-browser test reproduced Driver's 403; a PHP test then found role-permission caches stayed stale after `RolePermissionSeeder` synced grants. `RolePermissionSeeder` now grants the self-scoped preference permission to every seeded role and invalidates that role's cached permission set. The regression primes stale caches, checks reads for all 16 roles, then proves Driver cannot read or overwrite another user's preference. Red before fix; `NotificationValidationTest.php` passes 16/16 (81 assertions), including the new case (37 assertions); PHP syntax and `git diff --check` pass. Commit `bd983270`.

- Mold, capability, inspection-spec, inspection, and NCR screens had no server-authorized product lookup for their existing form permissions. Root cause is confirmed at the route boundary: `StoreCollectionRequest` accepts the key as nullable while `InvoiceService` only replays safely when one is supplied.

Read-only DB state confirmed 2 collections totalling ₱2,100, 2 posted collection journals, and 2 official receipts. This is a confirmed duplicate-payment/data-integrity defect. - Fix is implemented in the existing idempotency path: the API now requires a validated collection key; the SPA derives it from invoice identity plus current paid/balance state, so tabs and users acting on the same invoice state send the same key. No schema change is needed; the key column and unique constraint already exist. The API regression first failed with 201, then passed 7 assertions with no collection, journal, receipt, or balance write. `npm run lint` and `npm run typecheck` pass.

The cancellation API regression verifies the durable outbox payload has `activeStep=cancelled` and `completedSteps=[]`. Focused resolver, CRM, and PR run: 40 tests / 181 assertions. Commit `aa58d24a`.

#### Decision questions

1. **Scheduler-stale critical email recipients.** Scenario: `scheduler_stale` raises a critical alert, but no role is configured for its email fan-out. The code records a failed attempt and retries; it cannot infer who owns scheduler health. Options: (a) add `system_admin` as default (recommended; system-wide issue, existing role), (b) route to production operations roles (more operational visibility, unclear infrastructure ownership), or (c) define a dedicated operations/on-call role (clear ownership, requires role provisioning and staffing rules). Recommendation: (a), with the existing admin setting allowing site override. Interim guard: keep the critical alert durable and retry state recorded; the alert remains available to users with `alerts.view`. No workflow advances based on email delivery. No default role added pending decision.
2. **Refund or retained balance for a customer return after full payment.** Scenario: a return is raised against a paid invoice and its credit note is finalized with no other open invoice. Why unresolved: the customer credit can be applied only to another finalized or partially paid invoice; there is no cash-refund path, and business policy does not say whether to refund or retain the balance. Options: (a) add a maker-checker cash refund with a posted journal and audit trail, (b) retain the unapplied credit for a later open invoice (recommended interim; credit-note balance already supports this), or (c) require an external Finance settlement process. Tradeoffs: (a) needs payment-method, approval, and reconciliation rules; (b) may delay cash repayment; (c) leaves settlement outside the system. Interim guard: paid invoices cannot receive another credit application; the UI says when no eligible target exists, and no refund/payment is issued.
3. **Manual vehicle status transitions.** Scenario: a fleet manager moves a vehicle directly among available, in_use, maintenance, and retired; observed behavior accepts all 16 pairs when no scheduled or active delivery references the vehicle. Why unresolved: delivery transitions own in_use/available changes, but fleet managers also have a manual status selector and no explicit transition policy exists for maintenance, retirement, or reactivation. Options: (a) keep any transition under fleet.manage (flexible; can create statuses that disagree with dispatch), (b) make in_use delivery-owned and allow maintenance/retirement/recommission transitions (stronger consistency; needs a clear recovery path), or (c) add an audited vehicle-service lifecycle with separate maintenance and retirement actions (best traceability; broader work). Recommendation: (b), with a narrow audited admin recovery action. Interim guard: only fleet.manage users can change status, and status changes/archive are rejected while any scheduled, loading, or in-transit delivery references the vehicle.
4. **Vehicle asset and maintenance association.** Scenario: fleet needs a capital asset link, depreciation, or maintenance work order. Why unresolved: schema/model contain `vehicles.asset_id`, but API/UI do not let users set it and maintenance excludes vehicles. Options: (a) keep fleet records operational-only (smallest scope; asset accounting stays separate), (b) add only an asset selector (links cost records but no vehicle maintenance workflow), or (c) integrate vehicles with asset accounting and maintenance orders (complete traceability; broader permissions and lifecycle). Recommendation: (c) if fleet units are capitalized and maintained in this system. Interim guard: the API ignores `asset_id`; no false association or vehicle maintenance action is presented.
- Harness collision: one extra DB-backed `AmountInWordsTest` run overlapped the earlier suite against the same database and read while `RefreshDatabase` was resetting it (3 failures for missing currency setting). It performed no writes and is invalid evidence; do not run database tests concurrently against the suite DB.
- Other chains, every transition/permission matrix cell, all page/action combinations, jobs/reports/exports, broad security attacks, and accessibility/responsive coverage remain UNVERIFIED.

Mission: find every gap between appearance and reality (process defects + completeness
defects), fix with root-cause server-side enforcement, guard with regression tests.
Ground rules: observed behavior only; UNVERIFIED until exercised; never weaken tests;
test DB only (`ogami_test*`); reversible migrations only, called out in final report.

## 0. Recon map (Phase 0 — DONE 2026-09-29, from repo + running app)

- Stack: Laravel 11 (PHP 8.3) API + React 18 SPA, PG16, Redis7, Reverb WS. Nginx :80
  serves SPA + `/api/v1/*`. Auth: Sanctum SPA cookies (no bearer). IDs: HashIDs.
- API: 22 modules, ~1052 route decls (`api/app/Modules/*/routes.php` → `/api/v1`),
  208 models, 183 enums, 212 services. Global pattern:
  `auth:sanctum` + `feature:<module>` + `permission:<slug>`; backend enforces, frontend
  guards (`AuthGuard/ModuleGuard/PermissionGuard`) are UX-only.
- SPA: 399 page files, 135 `src/api/*` files, router in `src/App.tsx` + `src/routes/`
  (24 files). Every page lazy + guarded. Existing e2e (`spa/e2e/`, 25 specs) mocks ALL
  backend via `page.route()` — proves DOM only, NOT integration. This mission's harness
  (`spa/e2e-real/`, created Phase 1) hits the real backend with zero mocks.
- Backend suite: 627 `*Test.php` files (phpunit, `DB_DATABASE=ogami_test`).
- Cron: 55 entries in `api/routes/console.php`. Events→listeners explicit in
  `AppServiceProvider::boot()` (~60). Roles: 16 seeded slugs, dev DB has 18 users
  (one per role, password `password` via DemoAccountSeeder). Workflows: 17 seeded,
  10 active (`leave_request, cash_advance, company_loan, purchase_request,
  purchase_order, bill_payment, salary_adjustment, return_request,
  finance_only_return_request, asset_disposal`); `payroll, work_order, ncr, 8d_report,
  separation_clearance, maintenance_request, department_transfer` are RESERVED/inactive.
- Chains: C1 Order-to-Cash (SO→MRP→WO→in-process QC→outgoing QC+CoC→Delivery→Invoice→
  Collection→GL), C2 Procure-to-Pay (PR→RFQ→PO→Shipment→GRN→incoming QC→Stock→Bill+3-way→
  Payment→GL), C3 Hire-to-Retire (Hire→Shift→biometric CSV→DTR→Leave/OT+Loans→Payroll→
  Payslip/Bank→GL→Separation→Clearance→Final pay). Full transition/enforcement detail
  per subagent recon (see session notes 2026-09-29).
- Prior audit posture: F-001..F-038 mostly verified-closed; F-030 OPEN (no retained
  production-like restore/deploy run); F-032 mitigated (narrow-browser proof absent).
  Known enforcement gaps to verify (not assumed): invoice-without-delivery gate (F-007),
  silent `transitionTo` no-op (F-017), PO `received` pre-QC (F-014), manual bill without
  GRN (F-018), no-BOM WO path (F-016), RMA credit without lineage (F-008).

## 1. Test harness (Phase 1 — DONE 2026-09-29)

- `spa/e2e-real/` — real-backend Playwright config (`playwright.real.config.ts`,
  baseURL `http://localhost`, headless Chromium, NO `page.route` mocks).
  - `helpers.ts`: login-as-role (API cookies + XSRF header + Referer/Origin for
    Sanctum stateful), console-error + failed-request collectors, benign-noise filter.
  - `auth.roles.spec.ts`: 17/17 PASS — UI sign-in + all 16 role sessions + wrong-pw
    rejection, zero console errors, zero server errors.
  - `crawl.spec.ts`: per-role route crawl (170 static routes), records console errors,
    HTTP failures, blank/404 pages → `e2e-real/results/crawl-<role>.json`.
- PHP adversarial chain tests: `api/tests/Feature/E2E/*` (own DB `ogami_test_e2e`,
  RefreshDatabase) — pending.
- Commands:
  - `docker compose exec -T spa npx playwright test -c playwright.real.config.ts --project=real-chromium` (headless)
  - `docker compose exec -T -e DB_DATABASE=ogami_test_e2e api php artisan test --filter='E2E'`
- Harness lessons (repo-relevant, not app defects):
  - `page.request` sends no Origin/Referer → Sanctum treats calls as stateless
    (401/500). Harness sends both; real browsers always do.
  - XSRF: must echo `XSRF-TOKEN` cookie as `X-XSRF-TOKEN` header on direct POSTs.
  - Auth limiter is keyed IP+email (5/min each) — role matrix does not self-throttle.
  - Signed-out `GET /auth/user` probe 401s are expected browser noise, filtered.
  - `spa/package.json`: `@playwright/test`/`playwright` 1.60→1.63 (1.60 has no
    Ubuntu 26.04 browser build; 1.63 browsers were already cached on host).
  - Dev `ogami` DB was behind migrations (`0500_sidebar_peek`+); `migrate` applied.

## 3. Findings log (defect → severity → status → test → fix)

1. **`chain.outgoing_qc_missing` not in NotificationCatalog** — MEDIUM.
   `SweepMissingOutgoingQc` (scheduled repair sweep) sends notifications with
   this key, but the key was absent from `NotificationCatalog::defaults()`,
   so users could not see or mute it — violating the catalog-completeness
   invariant pinned by `NotificationCatalogTest` (which caught it).
   **FIXED:** added to the Chain 1 group (commit 0f076d3b); catalog test 7/7.
2. **Migration 0565 silently re-broke multi-product return inspections** —
   HIGH. 0565 (cancelled-slot reuse) rebuilt
   `inspections_non_outgoing_entity_unique` copying 0468's column list and
   dropped the `COALESCE(product_id, 0)` column 0506 had added; the second
   product of any multi-product RMA died with SQLSTATE 23505. Caught by
   `ReturnInspectionHandoffTest::
test_multi_product_return_stages_one_inspection_per_product`
   in the suite run.
   **FIXED:** migration `0566_repair_return_inspection_uniqueness_index`
   restores the conjunction (per-product keys + cancelled rows release their
   slot); chunk 055 re-run green (62/62); dev DB migrated.
3. **Bill-cancel approval leak — FIXED.** A cancelled bill used to let the
   step-1 payment approval SUCCEED (only the final, payout step refused). No
   money could move, but the dead bill accumulated approval bookkeeping — a
   payment on a cancelled bill could show one approver's sign-off in history.
   `BillService::approvePayment()` now refuses at EVERY step, before any
   approval record is written (the final-step guard stays as the locked-row
   race backstop). Pinned by `ProcureToPayChainTest` step-1+final 422 probes
   plus a no-approver-written assertion.
4. **Duplicate NCR on a failed inspection 500s — FIXED.** Migration 0478's
   `ncr_inspection_unique` (one NCR per inspection, ever) had no domain-rule
   mirror: a second NCR POST for the same inspection died as an uncaught 23505
   → HTTP 500. `NcrService::create()` now refuses duplicates as a 422 business
   rule (constraint stays as the concurrency backstop). Pinned by the
   QualityGate chain test.
5. **Use-as-is concession could be self-granted by the failing inspector —
   FIXED.** Incoming-QC staging pre-assigns `inspector_id` = the warehouse
   RECEIVER (TriggerIncomingQC), while the QC inspector only authors the
   failing result. The concession guard compared only `inspector_id`, so the
   real failing inspector could grant their own use-as-is. Now blocks both
   the assigned inspector and any `result_authors` row (same predicate
   `InspectionService::review()` uses). Pinned by the QualityGate chain test.
6. **Scheduled delivery had no Cancel action — FIXED.** API allowed an
   authorized operator to cancel before dispatch and release unconsumed
   reservations, but detail UI exposed no action. Added role-gated cancellation
   with confirmation. `AUDIT20261032` proved QC's direct PATCH returns 403
   without writes; ImpEx canceled a real scheduled delivery, released its hold
   once, left on-hand unchanged, wrote the audit, and created no invoice,
   movement, or received quantity. ImpEx recreated the same SO line, inspected
   lot, and quantity through the form; replacement completed O2C. No migration.

## 4. Questions for owner (business rule ambiguous — behavior left unchanged)

1. **Quality verdict-only capture (WIP in tree, NOT mine):** LotResultPanel
   PASS/FAIL buttons + InspectionService/CoCService relaxation allow recording
   an inspection verdict without actual measurements on critical rows. This
   contradicts the CLAUDE.md rule "Outgoing QC: AQL 0.65 Level II. Actual
   measurements for critical dimensions" and weakens the IATF 16949 story that
   is the thesis differentiator. Verified consistent locally (56 quality+auth
   tests pass), but flagged as an owner decision, not silently reverted.
2. **Sidebar peek preference (WIP in tree, NOT mine):** migration 0500 + User
   model/request/resource/factory + SPA Sidebar/Topbar/AppLayout/auth.ts add a
   `sidebar_peek` user preference. Consistent and tested (spa tsc clean,
   UserPreferencesTest green); listed for visibility only.
3. **PG shared-memory lock ceiling (infrastructure, not app defect):**
   concurrent RefreshDatabase streams each run `migrate:fresh` (DROP of ~190
   tables). 7 parallel streams → `SQLSTATE[53200] out of shared memory /
   max_locks_per_transaction` → mass "failed (0 assertions)" carnage. **4
   streams also hit it.** PG limits deliberately not raised (out of scope);
   suite re-run on 2 streams instead (c1/c2 logs `/tmp/r1.log`, `/tmp/r2.log`
   in-container).
4. **Bill-cancel polish (P2P test, not fixed):** a cancelled bill still lets
   step-1 approval succeed (only the FINAL approval dead-ends). Harmless
   (payment is still refused) but the state machine is more permissive than
   the UI implies. Left as-is; noted for the owner. → RESOLVED since: see
   Finding 3 — the permissive step-1 path was hardened to refuse at every
   step (owner-approved polish, commit 09611d6f).



## 5. Chain-test contract notes (hard-won, for future test authors)

### Business-process audit (`BusinessProcessAuditTest`, 6/6, 132 assertions)
- **Payroll disbursement walk:** hr creates + computes, resolves anomaly flags
  (`payroll.anomalies.review` — hr holds it, finance does NOT), finance approves
  + finalizes (bank file + GL listeners fire) → mark-disbursed is REFUSED until a
  disbursement proof (file + `disbursement_date` + amount == payable net) is
  uploaded → then Disbursed. Re-disbursing 422s.
- **Bidding:** RFQ close-now is refused while invited suppliers have not
  responded; comparison ranks on allocated DELIVERED cost (freight can beat a
  lower unit price); award creates one draft PO per vendor and stamps
  invitation status awarded/not_awarded; second award 422s.
- **B2B:** cross-vendor PO access is 403 (not 404); acknowledge = accept-as-
  ordered → shipments allowed → receive needs the SEGREGATED flow (warehouse
  stages with qc.result=pending — warehouse lacks quality.inspections.manage so
  a terminal verdict 403s — QC maker records lot-result, independent checker
  reviews) → submit-invoice attaches the supplier invoice number to the
  auto-staged draft bill; a second invoice 422s.
- **Warehouse:** receive-goods fixes unit cost at the PO price (12.00 → 422
  "price differences are settled on the supplier bill"); UOM must match the
  item's (`pcs` ≠ `KG` without a conversion); stock-count sessions snapshot
  every stocked bin at start and EVERY line must be counted before completion;
  variance approval AND session completion are both maker-checker (creator
  cannot approve/complete their own).
- **Returns:** stockable customer returns need an inventory ITEM + SO-line
  provenance (`source_sales_order_item_id`) or the create 422s; inspect needs
  an active InspectionSpec (else manual_required); the staged return inspection
  must be answered (measurements) + completed PASSED before a restock dispose.
- **Forecasting:** manual override is upsert-per (product, customer, year,
  month); MRP projection honours `include_forecast_in_mrp` + is_active; the
  three `forecast.*` dashboard widgets (seeded by DashboardWidgetSeeder) are
  permission-derived — hr_officer/production_manager/qc_inspector/system_admin
  qualify via hr.employees.view / quality.view; finance via
  accounting.dashboard.view; purchasing_officer and employee do NOT (pinned).
- **Guard hygiene:** switching between the supplier_portal token guard and web
  users requires `$this->app['auth']->forgetGuards()` or the stale token 401s
  the next web request.

### Quality gate (`QualityGateChainTest`, 2/2)
- GRN incoming QC is **maker-checker**: the staged inspection starts Draft,
  `POST /inspections/{id}/lot-result` (checklist rows + `complete`) lands it
  at `awaiting_review`, and a SECOND user confirms via PATCH
  `/inspections/{id}/review` `{decision: passed|failed, remarks?}`. Reviewer
  = maker or any result author → 403. Only then do the chain listeners fire.
- Pass gate: `AcceptGrnOnIncomingQcPass` → `GrnService::settleIncomingQc()`
  accepts the pending_qc GRN. Fail gate: with MRB review on (default,
  `quality.incoming_failure.mrb_review`), the GRN is NOT auto-rejected — it
  HOLDS at pending_qc (`awaiting_mrb`) until the NCR's MRB disposition
  (`PATCH /ncrs/{id}/disposition`) settles it. use_as_is/rework → accept,
  return_to_supplier/scrap → mrb_accepted_quantity (default 0) kept, rest
  rejected. The MRB decision is final once the receipt settled against it.
- MRB decider needs `quality.ncr.manage` (qc_inspector holds it;
  production_manager does NOT — it only has inspections.review).
- The concession guard checks BOTH `inspector_id` and `result_authors` —
  staging pre-assigns the receiver as inspector, so comparing inspector_id
  alone misses the real failing inspector (fixed defect, Finding 6).
- Duplicate NCR per inspection → 422 (Finding 5).
- CoC is OUTGOING-only: GET `/inspections/{id}/coc` on an incoming
  inspection 422s `COC_STAGE_INVALID` even with a pass + full evidence.
- Wrong-time probes that pass: accept before QC decides (422), accept a
  terminal GRN (422), close NCR without disposition/CAPA (422), actions on a
  closed NCR (422), warehouse staff posting terminal QC results (403).
### Procure-to-Pay (`ProcureToPayChainTest`, 6/6, commit e21be332)
- Auto-bill listener `AutoCreateBillOnGrnAccepted` needs an automation actor:
  seed an active system_admin + `app(SettingsService::class)->set('system.
  automation.actor_roles', ['system_admin'])`.
- Bill-payment idempotency key rides the **`Idempotency-Key` HEADER**
  (`StoreBillPaymentRequest::prepareForValidation` merges header into body);
  collections instead take `idempotency_key` in the **BODY**. Inconsistent
  surfaces, both now pinned by tests.
- `withHeaders()` must precede `postJson()` on the actingAs chain —
  TestResponse has no withHeaders; postJson executes immediately.
- Self-approval guard is USER-level: the finance who records a payment can
  never approve it (ForbiddenActionException 403) — a second finance_officer
  is the step-1 checker. bill_payment chain = finance_officer → vice_president.
- "Header" account = any account with children (`PostingAccountResolver`
  checks `Account::where('parent_id', $account->id)->exists()`); posting to a
  header 422s "is a header account and cannot receive new postings" — tests
  pick leaf asset accounts (cashAccount() helper, whereNotExists subquery).
- Bill cancel is **PATCH** `/api/v1/bills/{bill}/cancel`; allowed while
  `amount_paid` is zero even with a pending reservation; refused once paid.
- Payment approval on a cancelled bill refuses at EVERY chain step (not just
  the payout): `approvePayment()` checks the locked bill state before writing
  any approval record. Note: `ApprovalService::submit()` PRE-CREATES the
  chain's pending step rows at submission — the audit invariant is "no
  approver_id/action written", not "no rows exist". `approvable_type` carries
  the FQCN (no morph map is enforced).
- Bill routes are `/api/v1/bills/...` (not `/api/v1/accounting/bills/...`).

### Order-to-Cash (`OrderToCashChainTest`, 5/5, commit 2a407e9a)
- SO create FormRequest resolves HashIDs (hashIdFields: customer_id, items.*.
  product_id) — send hash_ids, not ints. SO needs an active **PriceAgreement**
  per customer+product+delivery date else 422.
- `Tests\Support\ReceivesProductionOutput::dispatchableDelivery()` builds its
  own confirmed SO + WO + output + FG receipt + passed outgoing inspection +
  delivery; returns `[Delivery, Item, WarehouseLocation]`.
- Delivery walk: PATCH `/supply-chain/deliveries/{id}/assignment` (vehicle +
  driver + reason, Scheduled only) → PATCH `/status` loading → in_transit →
  delivered (jumping scheduled→delivered 422s) → POST `/confirm` is
  **proof-gated** (422 without proof; proof = multipart POST `/proofs` with
  `Storage::fake('local')`). Confirm auto-stages a draft invoice;
  re-confirm of a Confirmed delivery is a deliberate idempotent 200.
- Invoice finalize is delivery-gated (needs SO + Confirmed delivery else 422)
  and assigns `invoice_number`; double finalize 422s.
- Collections: POST `/invoices/{id}/collections`, model is
  `App\Modules\Accounting\Models\Collection` (NOT InvoiceCollection); full →
  Paid, partial → Partial, over-balance → 422; carry `journal_entry_id`.
- **refresh() trap (cost ~30min):** `Model::refresh()` re-loads all loaded
  relation NAMES; `show()` sets computed `preparation` via setRelation() which
  is NOT a real relation → subsequent refresh() throws
  RelationNotFoundException. In tests re-read via
  `Delivery::query()->findOrFail($id)`. App-side "fixes" were made then
  REVERTED — resource `$this->preparation ?? []` is already safe.

### Hire-to-Retire (`HireToRetireChainTest`, in progress)
- `hr_separation` is a permission MODULE, not a role — hr_officer holds
  `hr.separation.initiate` + `hr.clearance.sign` via `module('hr_separation')`.
  There is no separate separation role.
- Employee create: POST `/api/v1/hr/employees`, StoreEmployeeRequest needs the
  full field set (name regex letters-only, birth ≥15y, `^09\d{9}$` mobile,
  hash-encoded department_id/position_id, pay_type monthly →
  basic_monthly_salary required, date_hired ≤ today). Returns 201.
- Leave: POST `/api/v1/leaves/requests` — store authorize: filer must hold
  leave.approve_hr OR employee_id == user->employee_id (self). `submit()`
  requires pre-seeded `EmployeeLeaveBalance` rows per year ("Leave balance is
  not initialized for {year}"). Leave windows: `leave.request.past_window_days`
  (30) / `future_window_days` (365) seeded by migration 0318, NOT by
  SettingsSeeder. VL (default_balance 15, requires_document false) avoids
  document upload.
- Dept-head approval needs an Employee row in the SAME department
  (`assertDepartmentDecisionScope`); a holder of leave.approve_hr bypasses it.
  Wrong department → 403 (ForbiddenActionException). approve-hr before
  approve-dept → 422; double approve-dept → 422. Approved consumes the
  balance and marks attendance rows on_leave.
- Separation: POST `/api/v1/hr/employees/{employee}/separation` with
  `{separation_date, separation_reason, remarks}`; needs setting
  `hr.separation.clearance_checklist` (set via SettingsService). Refuses date
  < hire_date, already-separated, duplicate open clearance. Employee status →
  on_leave on initiation.
- Checklist item department labels resolve against the departments table
  (exact/prefix); an unresolvable label is HR-fallback-signer-only. signItem()
  sets item status **'cleared'** (not 'signed'); last item flips clearance to
  completed. Signing another department's item as a dept head → 422
  (BusinessRuleException from the per-department gate).
- Clearance routes are **`/api/v1/hr/clearances/...`** (inside the hr prefix),
  not `/api/v1/clearances/...`.
- Hire is a three-fixture chain, all synchronous/queued on EmployeeCreated:
  (1) `AutoProvisionUserOnEmployeeHire` creates the employee's login (role
  from `hr.default_user_role_slug` = employee, `must_change_password=true`);
  (2) `EmployeeService::create` seeds PRORATED current-year leave balances via
  `LeaveBalanceService::seedProratedFor` — tests must fetch the hire-created
  row, never insert their own (unique key);
  (3) `EmployeeWelcomeNotification` (mail) carries the temp password
  (`protected string $tempPassword`, readable via bound closure).
- The force-change gate is the `CheckPasswordExpiry` API middleware: ANY
  business call 403s with `code=password_expired` until POST
  `/api/v1/auth/change-password` succeeds (`current_password` = temp,
  StrongPassword rule). `/auth/user`, change-password, logout are exempt.
- Leave store requires `WorkflowSeeder` (leave_request chain) — submit() →
  `ApprovalService::submit` 404s without the workflow definition row.
- `leave_type_id` in API payloads is a HashID string (int fails validation:
  "The leave type id field must be a string.").

### Loans (`LoansChainTest`, 2/2, commit ca54c7a2)
- hr_officer raises loans (holds the whole loans module); employees can
  preview amortization unauthenticated-role but not request.
- cash_advance chain: department_head → finance_officer → vice_president;
  company_loan inserts production_manager as step 2. The VP is row-scoped via
  chain participation (`LoanAccessPolicy::isChainParticipant`), NOT global —
  only system_admin/finance_officer/hr_officer are global loan operators.
- **Two refusal layers with different statuses:** the service's own
  `canDecide` row-scope guard fires FIRST (422 BusinessRuleException — e.g. a
  production_manager on a cash_advance, where he is not a chain participant);
  ApprovalService's step-role guard fires second (403
  ForbiddenActionException — e.g. a dept-head double-tap, wrong-order step).
  Which status a wrong-actor probe gets depends on which layer sees it first.
- Loan payments carry the idempotency key in the **`Idempotency-Key` HEADER**
  — `RecordLoanPaymentRequest::prepareForValidation` OVERWRITES the body
  field with the header (same contract as bills, opposite of collections).
- Payments only on Active loans; amount ≤ ledger-derived balance; aggregates
  (total_paid/balance/status Paid) rebuilt from the immutable payment ledger
  under the row lock; replay returns the original payment row.
- GL (only when `modules.accounting=true`): disbursement = debit 1110
  receivable / credit 1020 cash (4020 interest line only if interest > 0);
  manual repayment = debit cash / credit receivable. Codes from
  ChartOfAccountsSeeder via `accounting.accounts.loan_*` settings.
- Write-off is finance-only maker-checker: `loans.write_off.request` (finance)
  then a DIFFERENT `loans.write_off.approve` holder (second finance; VP and
  dept head hold neither slug → 403). Requires Active status + positive
  balance + reason/evidence.
- EmployeeLoan model: `disbursement_journal_entry_id` (0538); LoanPayment FK
  is `loan_id` (not employee_loan_id); JE lines cast debit/credit decimal:2.
- Document sequences: LN-YYYYMM-NNNN (loan), CA-YYYYMM-NNNN (cash advance).

### MRP (`MrpChainTest`, 2/2, commit 7e6499c2)
- Confirming an SO dispatches `RunAutomaticMrpJob` (sync in tests) — the run
  row's `triggered_by` is **'automatic'** (the reason string
  'sales_order_confirmed' rides `summary`, not the trigger column).
- Full-shortage plan: `shortages_found=1`, `auto_pr_count=1`,
  `draft_wo_count=1`; one consolidated DRAFT auto-PR carrying the exact gross
  requirement; one planned root WO per SO line with BOM-exploded materials.
- Missing-BOM product: plan stays Active with a `missing_bom` diagnostic but
  produces NO PR and NO WO — demand visible, nothing ordered.
- Rerun reconciliation (stock now covers demand): new plan version supersedes
  the old, the planned root WO is REUSED and repointed to the new plan (never
  duplicated), and the stale draft auto-PR is CANCELLED — the purchasing
  queue never shows demand that no longer exists.
- `POST /mrp/runs` returns **202 Accepted** (ppc_head holds mrp.runs.trigger;
  sales_officer 403).
- WO confirm requires BOTH machine and mold (422), the mold must belong to
  the WO's product (422), and confirm RESERVES materials
  (`material_reservations` via Inventory). Double confirm → 409
  (IllegalLifecycleTransition). start() flips the machine to running.
- ppc_head authors BOMs over HTTP (`POST /mrp/boms`, hash-id product_id +
  items.*.item_id); the BomResource returns hash_id — decode it, never cast.
- Namespace traps: `Customer` is Accounting's (NOT CRM); WarehouseLocation
  has NO warehouse_id column (zone is enough for stock fixtures).

### Assets + Maintenance (`AssetsMaintenanceAuditTest`, 3/3, 54 assertions)
- Assets disposal is **two-phase** (AS-03): POST `/assets/{id}/dispose` only
  stamps proposal columns — status stays Active and NO JE exists. The chain is
  finance_officer (step 1) → vice_president (step 2); the 4-line JE (proceeds,
  accumulated-depreciation reversal, cost removal, loss/gain) posts only when
  fully approved. Self-approval is USER-level: the requesting finance gets 403
  on their own request — a SECOND finance officer is the step-1 checker. VP
  jumping to step 1 → 403 (ForbiddenActionException). Disposing a Disposed
  asset → 422. Disposal JE `reference_type` is the Asset FQCN; account codes
  come from SettingsSeeder (1010 cash / 1410 accum dep / 1400 cost / 6120 loss
  / 4030 gain).
- Depreciation: POST `/asset-depreciations/run` {year,month} (finance only;
  production_manager 403). StraightLine 12000/5y = 200.00/month; `runForMonth`
  asserts prior periods complete unless backfill. QR payload GET
  `/assets/{id}/qr` returns asset_code + self URL (assets.view: finance,
  maintenance_tech, ppc_head, production_manager, system_admin, VP).
- Namespace traps: `Machine`/`Mold` live in **MRP** (`App\Modules\MRP\Models\...`),
  NOT Maintenance — that module owns only WO/Schedule/usage models.
  `MachineDowntime` lives in **Production**.
- MWO create request DECODES the hashed polymorphic target itself
  (`StoreMaintenanceWorkOrderRequest::prepareForValidation` → HashIdFilter
  keyed on maintainable_type) — send `maintainable_id` as the Machine/Mold
  hash_id, never the int. Initial status `open` (enum: open / assigned /
  in_progress / completed / cancelled).
- MWO walk: create (maintenance.wo.create — maintenance_tech) → assign
  (maintenance.wo.assign — ONLY system_admin) → start/complete
  (maintenance.wo.complete). Start refuses 422 while the machine is running or
  has an active production WO, and the refused start must NOT advance the WO
  (the transition rolls back with the transaction). Start opens an OPEN-ENDED
  `machine_downtimes` row (maintenance_order_id, end_time null) and flips the
  machine to maintenance; complete closes the downtime (end_time set) and
  restores idle. Logs/spare-parts share the same gate; the spare-part item
  MUST be `item_type='spare_part'` else 422, and the issue draws down
  StockLevel at WAC (cost accumulates on the WO).
- Mold completion resets `current_shot_count` to 0 (+ MoldHistory
  maintenance_completed row) and never releases a mold production still owns.
- Schedules: POST/DELETE `/maintenance/schedules` gated
  `maintenance.schedules.manage` (only system_admin; prod manager 403 on both);
  interval hours|days|shots; delete is 204 soft delete. Downtime analytics
  (`/maintenance/downtime-analytics/*`) gated maintenance.view (prod manager
  reads). Condition-readings routes intentionally hidden (scope cut, no IoT).
- Wrong-actor probes that hold: employee cannot create MWOs (403), finance
  cannot start (403), production_manager cannot manage schedules or run
  depreciation (403). Regression: tests/Feature/Assets + tests/Feature/
  Maintenance suites 106 passed (484 assertions).

## 6. Full-suite runs

- 2026-09-29: recon only, no suite run yet.
- 2026-09-30 run 1 (4 streams, ogami_test_c1..c4): **lock carnage** — even 4

  ceiling (~815 `SQLSTATE[53200]` per stream, ~466 files failing mostly with
  "failed (0 assertions)"). Classified infrastructure, not regressions.
- 2026-09-30 run 2 (2 streams, ogami_test_c1/c2, logs `/tmp/r1.log`/`/tmp/r2.log`):
  **CLEAN at 2 streams except 5 chunks.** 10 chunks saw lock errors: 5 fully
  carnaged (r1: 017, 051; r2: 026, 028, 056 — the "failed (N assertions ≤ 5)"
  signature), 1 partial (058, 9 errors), 4 transient single-test 53200s (011,
  012, 039, 040).
- 2026-09-30 serial rerun of the 6 lock-affected chunks (`/tmp/rerun.log`):
  **466/466 passed, 0 lock errors** — all carnage was infrastructure, not
  regressions. The 4 transient files also re-ran green (38/38).
- **Real defects found by the suite run: 2** (see findings log) —
  `NotificationCatalogTest` caught the uncatalogable notification key;
  `ReturnInspectionHandoffTest` caught the 0565 index regression. Both fixed

- Suite total observed across runs: ~4,000 passing executions of the 1,900-
  test suite; the 2 real failures above were the only assertion failures
  anywhere — everything else carried the 53200/0-assertion carnage signature.
- Chunking note: `tests/Unit/ScheduledExportArtifactServiceTest.php` was
  bundled into chunk 063 (not isolated as planned) and still passed at 128M;
  the OOM co-runner condition did not materialize this run. Keep the

- 2026-09-30 FINAL CONFIRMATION run (all three fixes in: NotificationCatalog,
  0566 index, bill-approval guard; 64 chunks incl. the three E2E chain tests
  and the OOM-prone unit file isolated as its own chunk):
  parallel pass had 17 lock-affected chunks (streams started synchronized —
  chunks 1–12 collided in their `migrate:fresh` windows; every failing summary
  chunk had 53200 errors). **Serial rerun of those 17 chunks: 1,115 tests
  passed, 0 failed, 0 lock errors.** Across both logs: ~4,546 test executions
  passed. All three E2E chain tests PASS in the parallel run itself. The
  suite is green with all fixes in.
  - Operational lesson: two streams launched simultaneously stay
    synchronized long enough to collide on early chunks. A start offset
    (sleep 60–120 on stream 2) or inter-chunk jitter would reduce even the
    remaining transient carnage; serial rerun of affected chunks remains the
    deterministic fallback and classification rule (53200 + failing summary
    ⇒ infrastructure; any real failure shows real assertions and a specific
    test).

### Payroll follow-up — 2026-10-03 (Pass 1 still open)

- A real browser walk on the isolated runtime DB exercised the payroll chain from HR period creation through compute, Finance approval, distinct HR anomaly review, Finance finalization, bank-file/GL reconciliation, and System Admin void. Every action was followed by status, actor, audit, amount, bank artifact, and ledger assertions before advancing. The test also tried same-approver finalization and maker self-review through direct API requests and verified no records or effects were written. `payroll-period-void-chain.spec.ts` passed 1/1; its request and console failure filters were clean apart from deliberately asserted API gate responses.
- API RED runs reproduced: a period creator who changed into Finance could approve their own period (200); the approver could finalize the same period (200); a payroll maker could resolve their own anomaly (200); anomaly resolution accepted Draft/Processing/Finalized/Disbursed/Voided states (200); and an Approved legacy period without `approved_by` could finalize (200). Server guards now include requester/computer identity, anomaly lifecycle status, recorded approver, distinct finalizer, immutable resolution audit, and an audited correction path for malformed Approved data. New/updated maker-checker and anomaly tests pass in the current focused run; full Payroll rerun is pending.
- The real payroll detail page displayed a literal “Future enhancement” deduction placeholder. The detail response now aggregates statutory employee shares, withholding tax, loan/cash-advance deductions, signed net adjustments, and uncategorized amounts from payroll rows. A regression first failed on absent summary fields; it now passes. The browser sees the real breakdown and no placeholder, and independently sums raw row amounts back to the period summary.

API RED tests reproduced successful approval and finalization despite that inconsistent field (200). Approval and finalization now return 422 before writes, summary exposes the value, and UI explains the gate. No schema change.
- Added test-only `AuditPayrollReviewerSeeder` behind `AUDIT_E2E_TESTING=true`; seeded only the isolated audit DB. HR maker cannot resolve anomaly flags; another authorized HR user can, and every resolution records immutable before/after values. Repeated resolution returns 422 without audit or value changes.
- Focused PHP run: `PayrollMakerCheckerTest`, `PayrollAnomalyReviewTest`, `PayrollPeriodSummaryTest`, and `PayrollUnclassifiedDeductionTest` passed 18 tests / 128 assertions. Lifecycle/event, bank-file, GL-handoff, money-regression, and 13th-month follow-up tests passed 51 / 166. The complete Payroll rerun passed 352 tests / 1,075 assertions in 949 seconds on PHP's default 128 MB limit; no memory override was used.
- SPA typecheck and targeted ESLint pass. A full Vitest run passed 63 files / 385 tests twice. A later concurrent run failed one Finance sidebar assertion: `Procurement` was absent from the rendered modules (384 / 385); isolated `Sidebar.behavior.test.tsx` passed 7 / 7. A single-worker full rerun is pending. The response-interceptor test now stubs navigation and asserts `/sign-in`, removing jsdom navigation warnings; the fixed-size Recharts warning remains.
- Fresh Accounting API suite passed 284 / 296 tests; 12 requests returned 401. One reproduced customer-list response says `account_inactive` even though the test actor remains active in the database. Root cause unresolved; do not classify as infrastructure or change expected status until the request principal is identified. With other PHP suites finished, the isolated `DeliveryAttemptOutcomeServiceTest` passed 13 / 90; a serial per-file rerun of all 28 Supply Chain feature files is in progress. No memory-limit override was used.

#### Decision questions opened by this follow-up

1. **Finance maker/checker override:** Scenario: a user with explicit `payroll.periods.self_approve_override` created or computed the period and approves it. Why undecided: System Admin currently has wildcard permission, and older code intentionally allowed that override, but no business policy says when it is appropriate. Options: retain a logged exception; prohibit all maker approval; allow only a named break-glass role with a reason. Recommendation: restrict to explicit break-glass use with a required reason and audit marker. Interim: only the existing permission can override maker identity; the audit records `maker_checker_override=true`; no silent override.
2. **Payroll approver and finalizer identity:** Scenario: one Finance user approves and finalizes. Why undecided: lifecycle had separate steps but no policy that defined whether one person may hold both duties. Options: allow one Finance user; require distinct Finance users; require a second actor only above a threshold. Recommendation: keep two-person control until Finance sets limits. Interim: server returns 403 for same approver/finalizer; UI hides Finalize and explains the next actor.
3. **Anomaly reviewer independence:** Scenario: the HR user who created or computed the payroll attempts to clear anomaly flags, or no independent HR reviewer is available. Why undecided: the existing `payroll.anomalies.review` grant was not scoped by maker identity and no delegation or escalation policy exists. Options: any HR reviewer; distinct HR reviewer; Finance reviewer; audited System Admin recovery. Recommendation: distinct HR reviewer with logged admin recovery. Interim: makers are blocked; another user with review permission can resolve; System Admin wildcard does not bypass maker identity.
4. **`other_deductions` calculation:** Scenario: imported or legacy payroll row has nonzero `other_deductions`. Why undecided: current calculator writes zero, stored payroll total and net exclude it, and no writer defines whether it is a deduction, an adjustment, or obsolete data. Options: include it in deduction/net totals; require typed detail records; retire the field and reject all nonzero imports. Recommendation: require an explicit deduction type and effective calculation before using the amount. Interim: summary exposes the raw amount; approval and finalization return 422 with no side effects.

- Still unverified in this pass: complete Payroll transition and permission matrices; absent/deactivated approver recovery; role deactivation during approval; concurrency between finalization and flag resolution; every payroll page/role; Accounting API failures and their active-user 401 root cause; corrected full Admin rerun; full Supply Chain file-by-file run; full application suite. No memory-limit override was used.

Typecheck, ESLint, `git diff --check`, and SPA build passed. Single-worker SPA Vitest passed 70 files / 415 tests.

The first attempt also used a test DB that had not been created, which caused setup failures; the focused rerun passed once the isolated DB existed. The final PHP gate must run in smaller serial batches; do not classify this harness limit as an application pass or failure.

With the synchronous queue used by the audit app, a missing GRN billing actor leaves a failed outbox/listener run, not a pending row for the scheduled dispatcher; the accepted GRN remains intact without a partial bill. The recovery UI can replay only that listener after an authorized admin restores the actor, then record an audited resolution. The regression and live E2E for this path pass below. Movement GL posting records `manual_required` when account 5010 is unavailable and exposes replay through the movements UI. RED probes failed on those cases. `Item::canDelete()` now blocks any nonzero or reserved stock, pending adjustments, and items referenced by draft or in-progress counts. The regression test passes Pint, PHP syntax checks, and `git diff --check`. PHP memory stayed at its default; no migration.

#### Decision questions added by this continuation

5. **Inventory transfer value rounding:** Scenario: transfer a fractional quantity at a four-decimal WAC; summing per-location values rounded to cents changes the displayed total by one cent. Why unresolved: quantity and movement cost reconcile, but no Finance rule defines inventory value precision or rounding boundaries. Options: retain four-decimal WAC and round only at GL boundaries; store higher-precision location value; or round each transfer to cents and adjust WAC. Recommendation: retain precise quantity/WAC internally and round once at the accounting boundary, pending Finance confirmation. Interim: transfer preserves source cost and records its cost; test allows at most one cent in the sum of independently rounded location displays and checks exact movement cost.
6. **Stale CRM/Quality permissions:** Scenario: admin assigns a catalog permission with no endpoint or UI consumer (`crm.customers.manage`, `crm.so.create`, `quality.inspections.create`, `quality.inspections.edit`). Why unresolved: old role assignments and migration compatibility may exist outside the seeded configuration. Options: remove the slugs; map them to current permissions; or preserve them as inert legacy names. Recommendation: retire the four after checking deployed overrides and role assignments. Interim: route/controller checks use current slugs; the four catalog entries grant no action.
7. **Automation actor role for accepted-GRN billing:** Scenario: an accepted GRN needs an automatic draft supplier bill but no configured role has an active user. Why unresolved: safe recovery now exists, but no business rule settles which role may attribute automated billing. Options: require an active System Admin actor; permit Finance Officer; or provision a dedicated least-privilege automation role. Recommendation: use a dedicated automation role once one is approved. Interim: no partial bill is written; the failed outbox/listener run appears in recovery, and an authorized System Admin can restore an allowed actor, replay only the listener, and record an audited resolution.
8. **Pending stock-adjustment refusal:** Scenario: the Finance approver decides a pending adjustment must not post. Why undecided: the lifecycle only defines `pending` and `approved`; no rule says whether Finance rejects it, the requester withdraws it, or an admin recovers it. Options: checker-only reject with a required reason; requester cancel while pending plus checker reject; keep pending until approved or resolve through admin recovery. Recommendation: add checker rejection with a required reason and requester withdrawal before approval. Interim: no reject control or endpoint exists; direct attempts return 404, and pending adjustments remain visible without changing stock or audit state.

- Still unverified for Inventory: every state/action matrix cell and foreign-chain ID; partial transfer/return and cancel-after-transfer behavior; no-active-checker recovery; settings threshold boundary/rounding matrix; all Inventory pages and roles; repeated fractional WAC transfers; closed-period GL replay; real job/notification failure recovery. Purchasing, Quality, Production, Returns, Supply Chain, and remaining Accounting chains still need fresh pass-1 probes. No migration was added in this continuation.

#### Stock-count approval and completion follow-up — 2026-10-04

- Direct API RED: after a distinct Warehouse user approved a high-variance line, that same user completed the count and posted stock (HTTP 200). Completion now rejects any user who created the session, counted any line, or approved any line. Direct API regression passes 2 tests / 44 assertions; it verifies no status, stock, movement, or audit writes on refusal, then completes with a third actor.
- Direct API RED: recounting a verified line changed it back to `counted` but retained `verified_by`. `recordCount()` now clears the approval; completion refuses the changed high variance until an eligible checker approves again. Regression proves the second approval and final stock adjustment.
- UI now offers Recount for pending/counted/verified lines, prefills the prior count, hides variance approval from the creator/counter, and hides completion from the creator/counter/verifier with an explanation. A test-only Warehouse finisher was added to `AuditWarehouseCheckerSeeder` behind the existing `AUDIT_E2E_TESTING` guard.
- Real browser chain passed 2/2: exact count from creation through completion; high variance from creation through count, independent approval, recount after approval, re-approval, refusal of early completion through API, distinct final posting, stock quantity and movement reconciliation, audit actors, clean console and requests. E2E uses `ogami_test_audit_20261002`, separate from API tests.
- UI regression was run RED by reverting only the stock-count UI diff: the maker saw an `Approve` button (received 1, expected 0). After restoring the UI gates and recount action, the same real browser test passed 1/1.
- Actor disclosure RED: stock-count detail returned full `UserResource` records for creator, counter, and variance verifier, including email, role permissions, and features; the exact `{id,name}` regression failed. Session and line resources now serialize actor summaries only. The focused API test passes, resource/API integration and real browser chain reruns pass, and Pint passes on the resources.
- Empty-scope RED: an active location with no stock rows created a Draft with zero lines; Start returned 200 and entered an InProgress state that could never complete. Server now rejects Start transactionally and leaves status/audit unchanged. Draft can be cancelled in the UI; Start is disabled with a reason. API regression passes 1/16; the real UI test was RED on the enabled Start button and then passed after the guard and Cancel action (1/1).
- The first fresh full Inventory API run completed with 327 passing / 2 failing tests (1,374 assertions). Both failures were the legacy `CycleCountWacTest` fixture using its variance checker as final poster. Its WAC, quantity, and balance assertions remain intact; each now completes with a separate final actor. Focused WAC + SoD regressions pass 4 tests / 49 assertions. A full Inventory rerun is pending after remaining count-flow fixes.
- Single-worker SPA Vitest passes 70 files / 415 tests; `npm run typecheck`, targeted ESLint, PHP syntax, and `git diff --check` pass. No memory-limit override was used.
- DECISION QUESTION 9: who may finalize a stock count after line entry and variance approval when the warehouse has no third eligible person? Options: require a separate finalizer (strongest separation, can delay counts); allow the variance checker to post (two-person maker/checker, fewer staff); allow only an audited System Admin recovery (available fallback, admin workload). Recommendation: require a separate finalizer until Warehouse defines staffing/escalation. Interim guard: creator, any counter, and any variance approver are blocked server-side from completion; the UI explains the next actor requirement.
- DECISION QUESTION 10: how should a cycle count cover zero-on-hand items? Scenario: an active location has no `StockLevel` rows, so its count scope generates no lines and cannot represent unexpected physical stock. Why unresolved: code snapshots existing stock rows only; no rule says to enumerate all active catalog items or add ad hoc lines. Options: create lines for every active item/location (finds unrecorded stock, large counts); permit a counted item to be added ad hoc (flexible, needs valuation and identity controls); or limit counts to existing stock rows (smallest workflow, misses zero-to-positive findings). Recommendation: choose full catalog coverage if zero-on-hand verification is a control objective. Interim guard: empty scopes cannot start; cancel and choose a scope containing existing stock lines.

### Inventory UOM integrity — 2026-10-04 (Pass 1 in progress)

- Real edit-page probes found lower-case seeded UOM values (`pcs`) showed a blank select in both Inventory Item and CRM Product editors after the asynchronous options loaded. The real-browser regressions failed before the form-value reapplication/canonicalization fix and pass 2/2 after it; select values now resolve to the matching active catalog code.
- Direct API RED probes found lower-case item UOM submission failed validation (422) despite matching the active catalog, referenced UOM code edits/deletes succeeded, an unknown conversion UOM hash reached a foreign-key error, and a conversion targeting a unit other than the item base UOM was accepted. Requests now normalize and validate against active catalog codes, referenced codes cannot be changed or deleted (including when referenced by soft-deleted Items/Products), conversion writes lock their Item and UOM rows and require the Item base UOM as target, and missing IDs are rejected before database writes. API regressions cover the failed writes and unchanged state.
- Factor boundary RED probes found `0.0000001` was accepted (201) then stored as `0.000000`, while `1000000000000` escaped request validation and raised a PostgreSQL `numeric field overflow` (500). The conversion request now normalizes harmless leading/trailing zeroes and rejects factors that exceed the schema's 12 integer / 6 decimal precision. Both red tests pass (2 / 6 assertions); out-of-range requests leave conversion and audit rows unchanged.
- UOM catalog and conversion models now record audit changes. Name-only edits and removal of unreferenced UOMs remain allowed; code edits that would invalidate legacy references and deletion of referenced UOMs are blocked.
- Full Inventory API rerun passed **354 tests / 1,499 assertions** in 402.91 seconds on `ogami_test_fullpass2_20261004`. This includes `ItemUomStockIntegrityTest` and `UomConversionTest`; no database lock errors or PHP memory override. `CustomerProductPricingTest` passed 19 / 75 focused. Full CRM API rerun passed **143 tests / 486 assertions** in 338.49 seconds on separate `ogami_test_uom_crm_20261004`.
- Single-worker SPA Vitest passed **70 files / 415 tests**; `npm run typecheck`, `npm run audit:api-routes` (966/966 mapped requests; 14 explicit scope exceptions), `npm run audit:tokens` (853 files), and `git diff --check` passed.
- Running full Inventory and CRM migrations concurrently against separate test DBs caused PostgreSQL DDL lock/deadlock errors while both test suites rebuilt schemas. The lock-affected `AutoReplenishmentAttributionTest` passed 3 / 9 when rerun alone, and the complete Inventory suite then passed serially. Classify the parallel failure as harness contention, not an application assertion failure; keep migration-heavy PHP suites serial. SPA checks may run beside one PHP stream.
- Frontend reconnaissance found the backend exposes UOM list/create/update/delete/restore and per-Item conversion endpoints, but the SPA has only a list API wrapper and no UOM management route or conversion editor. This is a confirmed frontend completeness gap; add the smallest permission-gated surfaces using those existing APIs. No new business rule is needed for CRUD guards because the server already rejects referenced-code changes and referenced-UOM deletion.
- DECISION QUESTION 11 — changing an Item's base UOM after historical or open records exist: Scenario: the item's stock, movement, count, purchasing, BOM, or conversion records use its current base unit. Why unresolved: the current model stores a string UOM on historical documents and no migration/revaluation workflow defines how quantities should be transformed. Options: (1) keep the base UOM immutable after any such use and create a replacement Item with an audited stock migration (safest audit trail, duplicates item identity); (2) add an audited migration that rewrites every affected quantity and balance (preserves identity, high corruption risk); (3) permit a future-facing conversion while preserving old base quantities (flexible, requires every consumer to become conversion-aware). Recommendation: option 1 until Inventory defines and tests a migration policy. Interim guard: server rejects changing the base UOM while stock, movements, counts, open purchasing records, BOMs, or conversion rows reference it.

This was a fixture/configuration issue, not a UOM product failure.

The first stock-adjustment browser run could not select a location; after `WarehouseSeeder` it proved a zero-on-hand item has no `StockLevel` row. The test now asserts that pending creation and forbidden attempts preserve absence when no row existed, while still checking existing rows byte-for-byte when present. #### Open delegation decisions

- **DECISION QUESTION 12 — no eligible same-department approver:** Scenario: a department-head step is pending and no active colleague in that department can cover it. Why unresolved: the server correctly rejects cross-department cover, but no audited reassignment or escalation flow exists. Options: (1) audited System Admin reassignment to an active same-department role holder (preserves scope, may wait for an eligible person); (2) permit a named cross-department exception with explicit record scope (more flexible, higher authorization risk); (3) return or cancel the record (clear restart, lost time). Recommendation: option 1. Interim guard: create returns 422 for a cross-department department-head delegate; module scope still blocks out-of-department decisions; the pending step cannot be bypassed.
- **DECISION QUESTION 13 — approver deactivated mid-chain:** Scenario: the current approver is disabled or deleted while a record waits on their role. Why unresolved: delegations stop working when the delegator is inactive, and no step-reassignment or escalation process exists. Options: (1) audited admin reassignment to another eligible actor (preserves chain, requires distinct actor and scope checks); (2) automatically advance to the next role (can skip required review); (3) cancel and resubmit (restarts work). Recommendation: option 1. Interim guard: inactive users and their delegates cannot approve; the record remains pending rather than advancing without authority.
- **DECISION QUESTION 14 — delegate consent and notice:** Scenario: a delegator creates cover while the delegate is unaware or unavailable. Why unresolved: delegation activates immediately and no acceptance or notification step exists. Options: (1) notify and activate immediately (simple, may assign unwanted duties); (2) require delegate acceptance (clear consent, cover may remain pending); (3) require acceptance only for sensitive roles (more policy, uneven behavior). Recommendation: option 2. Interim guard: delegated authority remains limited to the delegator's current role, active accounts, existing maker-checker rules, and module scope; consent and delivery status remain unverified.
- **DECISION QUESTION 15 — reusing a revoked delegation window:** Scenario: a user intentionally needs to grant the same person and role for the same dates after revocation. Why unresolved: current API treats an exact retry of a revoked grant as a stale retry and returns 422; no explicit reactivation action exists. Options: (1) keep revoked rows terminal and require a new date window (clearest history, may need a wider window); (2) add an audited Reactivate action with a reason (supports legitimate reuse, requires approval policy); (3) accept another POST as reactivation (ambiguous and unsafe). Recommendation: option 2. Interim guard: a revoked grant cannot be reactivated by replaying POST; API names the conflict and leaves authority disabled.

**DECISION QUESTION 16 — company VAT status unset:** Scenario: a fresh deployment has not configured `company.vat_status`; `SettingsSeeder` intentionally leaves deployment identity empty, while every internal and portal layout requests `/business-policies`. Recommendation: option 3. Interim guard: the server refuses to return incomplete business policy and tax-sensitive services continue to require configured values; whether the SPA explains the block and the admin flow resolves it remains unverified.
