Fresh Return Management what-if probes queued for the next audit pass (not yet run):

1. Department Head is deactivated after submission when no other active user holds that role; prove the RMA has an audited recovery path.
2. Production Manager is deactivated after step 1 commits but before step 2; prove the request remains pending and can be reassigned or safely blocked.
3. The requester gains the Department Head role after submission and attempts to approve their own request; verify identity-based SoD still rejects it.
4. Department and role membership change while an approval is open; verify the current step is re-evaluated and no stale session can approve.
5. Step 2 races step 1's request while the first HTTP response is delayed; verify step 2 cannot observe or consume a half-committed transition.
6. Two RMAs concurrently claim the last available quantity on the same delivered source line; verify source allocation never exceeds delivered quantity.
7. Customer-return source-to-Item mapping changes after draft creation but before submission; verify stale client values cannot route quantity to another Item.
8. Customer or Product is archived after physical receipt but before credit-note finalization; verify safe completion or an actionable hold.
9. Inspection spec is revised after an Inspection is staged but before QC measurement; verify the existing Inspection retains an unambiguous spec revision.
10. A multi-product RMA has one valid spec and one missing spec; retry after repair must reuse the first Inspection and create only the missing one.
11. Quality spec is archived after measurements but before independent review; verify QC cannot accidentally review incomplete or mismatched criteria.
12. RMA is cancelled after a partial quarantine receipt, then again after a restock movement; verify each downstream quantity is reversed exactly or cancellation is blocked with a reason.
13. Two Finance Officers finalize the same return credit concurrently; verify one posted journal, one balance change, and no duplicate credit.
14. A source Delivery line is soft-deleted after RMA draft creation; verify submission and receipt cannot turn an orphaned source into accepted inventory or credit.

### Decision Question 18 — Reinspection after a failed QC proposal

- **Scenario:** an outgoing inspector records failed evidence, then a checker receives sorting or rework evidence before finalizing the inspection.
- **Why unresolved:** the service blocks changing a failed proposal to PASS and directs users to a linked NCR. The Quality guide says PASS cannot contradict saved failed evidence, but does not say whether verified rework can revise the same inspection or requires a separate reinspection.
- **Options:** (1) keep the failed inspection immutable, record sorting or rework through its NCR, and create a linked reinspection; this preserves the original evidence but adds a document and step; (2) allow the checker to pass the same inspection after linked NCR evidence is recorded; this is shorter but can make the failed sample appear to have passed; (3) permit a QA lead waiver with a reason and audit trail; this handles exceptions but expands privileged override power.
- **Recommendation:** option 1, with a new linked inspection after NCR disposition.
- **Interim guard:** the server rejects PASS on a failed proposal before any review, terminal-status, or audit write. `InspectionMakerCheckerPolicyTest::test_checker_cannot_override_a_failed_proposal_to_pass` verifies the unchanged pending state. Guard remains until policy is confirmed.

### Pass 1 follow-up — pending and approved RMA cancellation (2026-10-03)

- Transition-matrix audit found that the server permits `pending_approval -> cancelled` and untouched `approved -> cancelled`, while the detail page offered Cancel only for drafts. `ReturnRequestService::cancel()` already enforces the safety boundary: no cancellation after physical receipt or stock effects; source allocations are released; pending approval rows are superseded; approved rows lose current status.
- Red browser run `PASS1RETCANCEL1` reached the new pending-approval scenario through Customer Service's create and submit UI, read `pending_approval` with two pending approval rows and no receipt or inspection, then failed because no Cancel button existed. This confirmed a frontend dead end, not a missing server transition. Command: `COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME=ogami-audit-20261002 RETURN_TEST_BOOTSTRAP=1 RETURN_TEST_PHASE=quality-recovery RETURN_RUN_ID=PASS1RETCANCEL1 RETURN_TEST_OUTPUT=/tmp/ogami-return-qc-cancel1 RETURN_API_PORT=8240 RETURN_SPA_PORT=5240 node scripts/return-management-headless.cjs`.

Decision Question 19 — self-void of a posted bill payment

- **Scenario:** payment creator also has `accounting.bills.void_payment` and attempts to reverse their posted payment.
- **Why unresolved:** repository rules require maker/checker for approval, but do not explicitly define whether voiding is a conflicting duty.
- **Options:** (1) allow any authorized Finance user, including creator, to void; fastest recovery but no independent check; (2) require a different authorized user; preserves separation with one extra Finance actor; (3) require supervisor approval; strongest control but adds workflow and stuck-state risk.
- **Recommendation:** option 2, consistent with existing maker/checker controls.
- **Interim guard:** server returns 403 `A different authorized user must void this payment.`; UI hides the action and shows that reason. Distinct Finance user can void with balanced posted reversal. Awaiting business decision.

Decision Question 20 — production output during a closed accounting period

- **Scenario:** parts are physically produced, but the finished-goods receipt would post to a closed accounting period.
- **Why unresolved:** the API currently returns 422 `2026-07 is closed` and rolls back the output row, produced/good counters, and mold-shot increment. The repository does not define how to preserve a physical production fact without posting into a closed period.
- **Options:** (1) keep production output blocked until Finance reopens the period; preserves period control but leaves produced parts unrecorded; (2) record the physical output and hold its inventory/GL receipt as `manual_required` for reconciliation into an authorized open period; preserves production evidence but temporarily separates output from stock; (3) backdate the receipt into an open period; keeps the chain moving but misstates posting dates.
- **Recommendation:** option 2, with the stock/GL handoff visibly blocked until a Finance-approved reconciliation posts.
- **Interim guard:** the server rejects the output with 422 and performs no partial writes. Physical output requires reconciliation outside this record-output transaction until policy is decided.

4. All confirmed repair pieces are rejected; scrap movement and GL must balance, and no production receipt should be created.

6. Production user's role changes between sorting, repair start, output, and finish; server must re-check current permissions at each step.
7. NCR disposition changes after repair start; prevent an orphaned equipment reservation and stock movement.
8. Product QC specification changes between original inspection and repair reinspection; inspection must retain an unambiguous criteria revision.
9. Account mapping disappears between repair consumption and repaired receipt; transaction must roll back both effects or leave one durable, balanced recovery state.
10. A stale QC tab tries to close or cancel the NCR after repair output but before reinspection; no repair quantity may become untracked.
11. Lot expires between repair output and fresh QC approval; release must stop with an actionable hold.
12. Repair machine reaches maintenance threshold on final output; reservation, shot count, and repair completion must stay consistent.

### Decision Question 19 — In-process QC independence

- **Scenario:** A QC inspector records and submits a passing in-process inspection for a started work order.
- **Why unresolved:** `Inspection::requiresMakerChecker()` allows in-process QC to finish with one authorized result author, while the work-order release gate previously required independent checker fields. The real UI could save PASS, but output remained blocked. The code therefore disagreed about whether this QC stage requires an independent review.
- **Options:** (1) Keep in-process QC single-actor and require an audited result author before release; this preserves the current QC flow but has less SoD separation. (2) Require an independent review for every in-process result; this strengthens SoD but adds a queue step and requires a second eligible QC actor. (3) Add a privileged waiver with a reason; this handles absent reviewers but expands override authority.
- **Recommendation:** Option 1, because the existing Quality workflow treats in-process results as single-actor while outgoing QC requires a checker.
- **Interim guard:** A terminal in-process status releases production only when it has a recorded result author or valid independent checker evidence. A status-only PASS remains blocked. Failed inspections still require full-batch, independently approved NCR recovery. `WorkOrderProcessQualityGateTest` and the browser chain exercise these rules.

### Pass 1 follow-up — bill cancellation during pending payment approval (2026-10-04 UTC)

- The live UI chain created a ₱1.00 payment request as Finance, verified it pending with no payment journal, approved step 1 as a separate Finance actor, then verified step 2 pending, the unchanged bill balance, and no payment journal before any later action.
- A stale Finance page and a second Finance session cancelled the unpaid bill concurrently through the real UI and direct PATCH API. Both returned 200. The bill became cancelled with zero balance; its ₱672.00 payable journal was reversed exactly once with a balanced posted reversal. The request stayed pending and appeared in the VP action queue.
- VP approval after cancellation returned 422, “Cannot approve a payment on a cancelled bill.” Re-reading the bill and approval rows confirmed the rejected attempt made no changes. VP then rejected the request through the UI; it became terminal rejected, generated no payment journal, disappeared from the action queue, and left the bill cancelled. No pending payment remained.
- Red browser run: two prerequisite chains passed; the new test failed only because the cancelled bill still displayed Approve. The test completed VP rejection before reporting the UI failure, so test data did not remain pending.
- Root cause and fix: BillDetailPage rendered Approve and Reject together for every actionable pending payment. It now hides Approve for a cancelled bill, keeps Reject available, explains that rejection is the recovery action, and changes rejection-dialog copy to preserve the cancelled state. Bill cancellation remains available because observed service behavior intentionally permits it and the existing API chain test verifies approval is blocked afterward.
- Green browser command: E2E_BASE_URL=http://localhost:18080 npx playwright test -c playwright.live.config.ts -g 'PR to accepted GRN recovers one draft bill after automation actor loss|post supplier bill and approve its partial payment|cancels an unpaid bill and routes its pending payment to rejection' --reporter=list --output=/tmp/ogami-audit-payment-cancel-green4. Result: 3 passed in 3.2 minutes. The UI assertions cover cancellation confirmation, reversed journal totals/lines, concurrent repeat cancellation, approval guard, VP queue, recovery copy, and final rejection.
- Targeted API coverage: ProcureToPayChainTest.php, 6 tests / 120 assertions passed on isolated ogami_test_accounting_20261003. PHP default memory limit unchanged.
- Browser run diagnostics found no page errors, failed requests, or unexplained 5xx. The two local.ERROR outbox messages came from the test's deliberate removal of the automation actor; each accepted GRN was recovered by restoring the actor and replaying the durable event. This is an asserted fault-recovery scenario.
- SPA verification after the UI fix: 77 files / 430 tests passed; npx tsc --noEmit and targeted ESLint passed. No schema change or migration. Full API suite remains active on isolated ogami_test; latest observed progress was 35%.

Decision Question 21 — handling an in-flight bill payment when its bill is cancelled

- Scenario: Finance cancels an unpaid bill while a payment request awaits VP approval.
- Why unresolved: the system already allows cancellation, refuses later payment approval, and leaves the payment request for an explicit VP rejection. Repository rules do not say whether cancellation should also reject the payment automatically.
- Options: (1) auto-reject every pending payment when cancelling the bill; fewer actions, but cancellation silently records an approval decision; (2) keep cancellation and require the next eligible approver to reject; preserves explicit maker-checker history, but requires follow-up; (3) block cancellation until all payment requests are resolved; preserves the queue, but prevents cancelling an otherwise unpaid bill.
- Recommendation: option 2. It matches the verified current flow and keeps rejection attributable to an eligible approver.
- Interim guard: the server returns 422 for approval on a cancelled bill without writes. The UI hides Approve, keeps Reject available to the next eligible actor, explains the blocker, and accurately describes the result.

New bill/payment what-if angles for the next pass (not run):

1. Race final approval against rejection of the same payment in two browser sessions; exactly one terminal action and at most one journal may commit.
2. Race bill cancellation against VP approval after Finance step 1; cancellation and payment posting must serialize without a reversed or duplicated journal.
3. Keep a stale bill page open while another user submits a pending payment, then cancel from the stale page after permission revocation; server must reject without writes.
4. Deactivate the only eligible next approver after Finance approval, then attempt cancellation; queue must show the blocker and cancellation must not hide it.
5. Post a partial payment, create a second pending request, then attempt cancellation; posted money guard and pending-request guard must leave the posted balance and journal unchanged.
6. Void a posted payment while another request is pending, then race a stale cancellation; restored balance must not let cancellation orphan the request.
7. Deactivate the selected cash account after payment submission but before final approval, then reject and retry with a new eligible account; no duplicate reservation or journal may survive.
8. Reject a payment at the final step with a blank or oversized reason through UI and API; validation must preserve the pending state and keep the item actionable.
9. Submit cancellation twice after all pending requests are rejected; one cancellation audit record and one balanced reversal must exist.
10. Close the bill's accounting period after payment step 1, then attempt cancel and final approval; each result must preserve the current ledger and expose a valid recovery route.
11. Change a Finance user's department and role after a pending request appears, then use the same session to cancel and approve; fresh server permissions and actor separation must apply.
12. Deactivate the vendor while its bill has a pending payment; bill, queue, rejection, and audit history must retain usable lineage.

### Pass 1 follow-up — operational asset association lifecycle (2026-10-04 UTC)

- Confirmed defect: deleting a machine, mold, or fleet vehicle with a linked asset archived the operational row but left its asset foreign key. The relation then disappeared from ordinary asset queries; trying to replace it hit the existing uniqueness constraint as HTTP 500. Deleting or requesting disposal for an associated asset also bypassed the operational-record lifecycle and could strand disposal approval.
- Red evidence before the fix: linked vehicle archive returned 204 instead of 422; associated asset deletion returned 204; a legacy archived vehicle relation serialized as null; replacement produced a unique-constraint HTTP 500; linked disposal request returned 200. Focused regression cases encoded these paths.
- Fix: operational archive now locks and refuses linked rows until the asset is cleared. Asset relations and guards include soft-deleted operational rows, expose archived links for repair, reject deleting or disposing linked assets, and refuse new links while disposal is pending. UI shows archived links, keeps the clear action available, explains blocked disposal/archive, and restores allowed actions only after the server confirms clearing. No schema change or migration.
- API evidence: Assets shard passed **68 tests / 429 assertions**; updated `AssetAssociationTest.php` passed **11 / 90**; `AssetDisposalApprovalWorkflowTest.php` passed **13 / 75**. These run in test databases only.
- A fresh Inventory rerun exposed two stale test-fixture errors in `MaterialReturnServiceTest`: its QC helper tried to submit a lot checklist through `recordItemResults(submit=true)`, which the server correctly rejects without coverage. It now saves decisions, then submits sample coverage and the zero-defect result through `recordBatchResult`. The affected class passed **10 / 70**; after that red-to-green fix, the full Inventory shard passed **380 / 1,681** using PHP CLI `memory_limit=-1`.
- HR and Leave isolated API shards passed **343 tests / 1,611 assertions** while Inventory ran against a separate test database.
- Full browser rerun `O2CUI20261004N` walked the order, production, QC, dispatch, proof, customer confirmation, invoice, collection, and asset-link chain through the actual role UI, checking persisted state after each step. The asset gate was also attacked directly: UI and API archive both rejected a linked vehicle without changing association or audit state; Finance cleared it; archive then succeeded. Result: **350 checks passed**, **0 failures**, **0 5xx**, **33 expected API rejection console records**, **0 unexplained browser-console errors**, **0 page errors**. The **123,372-byte** server log had no error, warning, exception, or fatal lines.
- SPA verification after current changes: Vitest **80 files / 440 tests**, typecheck, full ESLint, production build, and `git diff --check` passed. Build reports one explained chunk warning: `NotFound.tsx` is statically needed by `PermissionGuard.tsx`, so Vite cannot isolate the duplicate dynamic fallback import from `App.tsx`; build succeeds. MRP shard passed **114 / 344** and Supply Chain passed **257 / 1,587** with PHP CLI `memory_limit=-1`.
- Overall audit remains incomplete. Runtime permission and transition matrices, most module role/page crawls, other chains, concurrency combinations outside exercised paths, and **80 dynamic route templates without seeded fixtures** remain unverified. No result here establishes production readiness.

New asset-association what-if angles for the next pass (not run):

1. Race asset linking against vehicle archive in two sessions; exactly one operation should win and neither may produce a 500 or partial audit.
2. Revoke the Finance clear permission after the detail page loads, then submit clear; the server must reject it and preserve the link.
3. Start disposal approval, then archive the linked operational row through a stale Fleet tab; the chain and foreign key must remain intact.
4. Clear a legacy link to a soft-deleted machine, then attach the asset to a mold; archived relation history must remain readable and the unique constraint must not leak as 500.
5. Race two users clearing the same association; exactly one state transition and one attributable clear audit should exist.
6. Re-associate a disposed asset using a stale form after disposal commits; API and UI must reject without changing disposal status.
7. Send a branch/company foreign asset ID to the association endpoint while the user can view the operational record; no foreign asset data or link may be exposed.
8. Deactivate a linked vehicle while disposal is pending, then attempt clear and final disposal; the record must retain an audited route out of the blocked state.
9. Archive an operational row and delete its linked asset in concurrent requests; locking must preserve the association rule in either commit order.
10. Load a long paginated asset list containing archived links; association status must stay correct without N+1 query growth or hidden legacy rows.
11. Retry association clearing after a client timeout with the original request; the link and audit history must remain idempotent.
12. Edit asset identity fields while another user clears its operational association; uniqueness, audit before/after values, and the relation must reconcile.

### Pass 1 follow-up — shipment Incoterm consistency (2026-10-04 UTC)

- Reproduced a commercial-document mismatch: the shipment create request accepted `DDP` for a PO recorded as `FOB`; shipment API/detail data stored `DDP`, while packing-list and commercial-invoice templates render the PO's `FOB`. The API returned 201 and created a shipment, audit row, and sequence number.
- The test first failed on the unexpected 201. `ShipmentService::create()` now rejects a conflicting explicit Incoterm before generating a number or writing shipment/audit rows. If omitted, the shipment inherits the PO term used by current customs documents. The regression also confirms an equal term is accepted. Focused result: **1 test / 10 assertions passed**; a full Supply Chain shard is running after the change.
- **Decision Question — which record owns the Incoterm for import documents?** Scenario: a supplier-sent PO has one Incoterm and the operator's shipment has another; legal trade documents currently print the PO value. Why unresolved: the shipment form and schema allow a separate term, but the generated customs templates use the PO and no workflow specifies whether shipment overrides are valid. Options: (1) treat the PO term as authoritative and inherit it on every shipment (consistent paperwork, cannot represent a legitimate shipment exception); (2) allow an audited shipment-level override and render that snapshot on shipment documents (supports exceptions, changes legal-document semantics); (3) remove the shipment-level field and maintain the term only on the PO (one source of truth, removes shipment-specific capture). Recommendation: option 1 until Purchasing confirms an override policy. Interim guard: the server rejects mismatches before any shipment, audit, or sequence write and inherits the PO term when omitted. The create form and shipment detail display remain to be verified in the browser.

### Pass 1 follow-up — fleet dispatch assignment and status gates (2026-10-04 UTC)

- Fleet probes found same-day scheduled deliveries could reserve one driver or vehicle twice. The later delivery then became un-loadable after the first assignment entered active dispatch. Tests first failed with HTTP 200 where 422 was required; a shared transactional assignment guard now rejects driver or vehicle conflicts on create, assignment, and reschedule before writes. Red/green focused API result: **5 tests / 83 assertions passed**, including state and audit immutability, all 16 vehicle status cells, and reschedule conflicts.
- A vehicle's plate or name could be edited after any delivery referenced it, retroactively changing historic delivery detail. API regression first failed with HTTP 200. Vehicle identity edits now reject once any normal or archived delivery references the vehicle; test confirms delivery detail and audit rows stay unchanged. The same focused API run passed.
- Directly changing an available vehicle to `in_use` bypassed dispatch; the UI also exposed that value as an ordinary manual status. API matrix test first failed with HTTP 200. The server now rejects manual entry into `in_use`, while dispatch retains ownership; the fleet editor omits that option and explains it. A second red case found `POST /vehicles` still accepted `status=in_use` with HTTP 201; `VehicleController::store()` now rejects it before writes. The full 16-cell API matrix plus create gate passes **1 test / 42 assertions**.
- **Decision Question — should dispatch assignments use day-level reservations or time windows?** Scenario: one driver/vehicle is assigned to separate scheduled deliveries on the same date. Why unresolved: current delivery data has a date but no scheduled time or duration. Options: (1) reserve each resource once per date (safe but reduces capacity); (2) add dispatch time windows and reject only overlaps (supports multiple runs, requires schedule UX/data); (3) permit repeated reservations and let loading decide (allows user-created blocked work). Recommendation: option 1 until scheduling data and policy exist. Interim guard: same-date duplicate reservations are rejected on the server across create, assignment, and reschedule.
- **Decision Question — how should vehicle identity corrections work after dispatch history exists?** Scenario: a referenced vehicle's plate or display name needs correction. Why unresolved: delivery records reference the live vehicle and have no identity snapshot or correction workflow. Options: (1) keep identity immutable after first reference (preserves history but requires a replacement record for corrections); (2) snapshot identity on every delivery (preserves old documents but requires schema/API/UI changes); (3) add an audited correction workflow with before/after history (allows fixes but changes historical display semantics). Recommendation: option 1 until Fleet and Finance define audit/document policy. Interim guard: the API blocks plate/name changes after any delivery reference, including archived deliveries.
- **Decision Question — who owns vehicle status transitions outside dispatch?** Scenario: an operator changes `in_use` or another fleet status without a delivery event. Why unresolved: the code has a fleet status editor, while active-delivery transitions also update status; no policy says which non-dispatch statuses require maintenance/Admin authority. Options: (1) derive `in_use` only from active deliveries and retain manual maintenance/availability edits (keeps existing fleet operation); (2) derive every status from delivery and maintenance records (consistent but needs a maintenance integration); (3) permit audited Fleet/Admin overrides (operational flexibility, risks divergence). Recommendation: option 1. Interim guard: normal API requests cannot enter `in_use`; active delivery status changes remain blocked; all other currently accepted status cells are unchanged pending the policy decision.
- The first full Supply Chain API shard reached **264 tests / 1,779 assertions**, then errored when `RefreshDatabase` hit a PostgreSQL deadlock while dropping tables for `AutoInvoiceOnDeliveryConfirmTest`; no product assertion failed. That class passes alone (**6 tests / 34 assertions**); a full shard rerun is in progress. Cause of the transient second database lock holder remains unverified.
- The first full real-browser run completed **181 checks: 177 passed, 4 failed**. All four failures used stale selectors against `SearchableSelect` or expected an ID in the visible category label. Corrected the E2E controls to interact with the real listboxes and displayed value; their focused rerun passes **10/10 checks**. The real-browser config pins one worker because specs share one database and auth limiter. A four-worker experiment later hit a login 429 and was interrupted; it is not counted as a full pass.
- `npm run test:run` passes **83 files / 456 tests**. `npx tsc --noEmit`, production build, targeted ESLint, Pint on the 9 Supply Chain API files, and `git diff --check` pass. The production build retains the already-recorded `NotFound.tsx` static/dynamic import warning. Pass 1 remains open; full Supply Chain API and full real-browser reruns are pending.

- **Decision Question — single-screen GRN endpoint scope.** Scenario: `/inventory/receive-goods` has an API client and server route, but no SPA caller; partial-accept and concession dispositions receive a clear 422 and must use the GRN partial-accept or supplier-return workflow. Why unresolved: no current UI or integration contract says whether external clients rely on this route. Options: (1) keep it as an API-only simple-receipt contract (smallest change; leaves an unused SPA wrapper), (2) build a UI for the supported simple path (adds a second receiving surface; still needs disposition policy), or (3) retire the route and wrapper after checking external usage (removes dead surface; risks undocumented clients). Recommendation: option 1 until API consumers are identified. Interim guard: inventory permission remains required and unsupported dispositions are rejected before writes with the supported workflow named.
- **Decision Question — export scope.** Scenario: export infrastructure is implemented and presented only for `hr.employees`; only that module has a registered exporter and SPA controls. Why unresolved: the system does not define which other lists need exports. Options: (1) retain HR-only (no fake controls; smallest surface), (2) add exports to selected modules with named owners (useful but requires per-module data/privacy rules), or (3) expose export controls on every list (broadly useful but risks sensitive data and incomplete implementations). Recommendation: option 1 until module owners define the data set and permission matrix. Interim guard: unsupported modules have no UI control and the server rejects them.

All ten failures were the same unresolved cross-module gate: customer-return restock/rework attempts a `Transfer` out of quarantine, while Inventory requires an MRB release by a different user with reinspection evidence. `ReturnRequestService` supplies no MRB reference or release evidence, and `QuarantineService::hold()` cannot attach an MRB to stock already received into quarantine. No behavior or test was weakened. The current 422 guard prevents the transfer and its partial effects; the RMA remains inspected with no release path for good-stock dispositions.
- **Decision Question 23 — resolved 2026-10-05.** User selected option 1: customer-return restock/rework remains quarantined under an MRB linked to the exact RMA line and receipt; a different eligible reviewer must record full-quantity reinspection evidence before release. The reviewer selects the good-stock location. RMA completion requires terminal MRB disposition and matching release movement. The prior interim guard is now the required workflow.
- Shipment state behavior conflicted: the prior API gate used `receivable()`, including `approved` and unresolved supplier-proposal states, while the SPA exposed only `sent`. The backend and focused status matrix now allow only `sent`, `acknowledged`, and `partially_received`; `approved` and unresolved proposal states are rejected before shipment, audit, or sequence writes.
- **Decision Question 22 — PO states eligible for shipment.** Scenario: shipment creation is requested before supplier dispatch, during supplier acknowledgement, or during partial receipt. Why unresolved: previous API and UI exposed different state sets. Options: (1) retain all `receivable()` states and expose them in the UI (broader support, permits pre-dispatch shipment); (2) permit `sent`, `acknowledged`, and `partially_received` (matches supplier dispatch and existing receipt progress); (3) permit only `sent` (strict UI contract, excludes accepted/partial orders). Recommendation: option 2. Interim guard: server and PO-options endpoint share that set; forbidden states create no shipment or side effects.
- Reproduced complaint outbox replay failure after a complaint row changed post-publication. `ComplaintNcrRequested` now opts into newer model state; the listener reloads and validates the current complaint and remains idempotent. The focused CRM/outbox run passed **7 tests / 22 assertions**. Full CRM coverage remains in progress.

The real-browser item catalog run passed **5/5** after the fix. Static RBAC scan now has **0 unseeded references** and still reports five catalog permissions without a scanned UI/API reference; `crm.view` is used by the dashboard-widget seeder, while legacy/stale entries still need role and migration review.

### Decision Question 24 — legacy CRM and inspection permissions

Scenario: `crm.customers.manage`, `crm.so.create`, `quality.inspections.create`, and `quality.inspections.edit` remain assignable in the permission catalog, but the live CRM and Quality routes/UI require newer scoped grants (`accounting.customers.manage`, `crm.sales_orders.create`, and `quality.inspections.manage`). `crm.view` appears only in dashboard-widget access among runtime references. A custom role carrying only a legacy grant may be blocked, while its grant label suggests usable authority.

Why unresolved: source and route behavior prove the legacy slugs do not authorize these actions, but no migration or compatibility rule defines whether old custom-role grants should be mapped, retired, or intentionally remain inert. Mapping `quality.inspections.edit` to `quality.inspections.manage` could expand authority.

Options: (1) migrate only explicitly approved legacy role grants to current scoped permissions, then retire the unused slugs (clear catalog, but requires exact grant mapping and migration); (2) keep the old slugs assignable but inert and document them as legacy (no privilege expansion, leaves misleading grants); (3) add compatibility checks mapping old grants at authorization time (preserves existing custom roles, but may grant broader access and adds a second policy path).

Recommendation: option 1 after permission-owner review of each grant mapping. Interim guard: current API and UI checks continue to require the explicit scoped permissions; legacy grants do not bypass server authorization, and no role or permission data is changed.

### Decision Question 25 — correcting a UOM conversion after use

Scenario: a conversion factor is wrong after purchase orders, receipts, or issues have used it. The API rejects factor changes and deletion; the item page shows the factor as locked. Historical and future quantities still depend on this same conversion row.

Why unresolved: the current guard preserves recorded calculations but offers no controlled correction or conversion revision workflow. A new UOM code is a manual workaround; the source does not define how corrected factors should affect future documents.

Options: (1) keep the old conversion immutable and create a replacement UOM code for future transactions (small change, but grows the catalog and requires users to remap); (2) add effective-dated conversion revisions and store each document's factor snapshot (preserves history and supports future corrections, but needs a reversible schema/data migration and a defined cutover); (3) allow an audited administrator restatement that recalculates affected documents and stock (supports correction, but has the highest risk to inventory and accounting history).

Recommendation: option 2 after Finance and Inventory owners confirm date and snapshot rules. Interim guard: API continues returning 422 for factor change/delete after use, the UI explains the lock, and no referenced factor or downstream record is changed.

### Decision Question 26 — configured CORS frontend origin

Scenario: the test API sends credentialed CORS responses for the configured frontend origin `https://47.129.228.44`, while the repository default is `http://localhost`.

Why unresolved: the allowlist is environment-driven through `FRONTEND_URL`; the repository does not establish who owns the public origin or whether it is the intended production frontend.

Options: (1) keep the single configured origin after its owner confirms it (least access, requires confirmation); (2) configure an explicit list for separately operated frontends (supports multiple clients, expands credentialed access); (3) allow a wildcard (simpler, but incompatible with credentialed CORS and unsafe for private API data).

Recommendation: option 1 unless the product owner confirms a second frontend. Interim guard: CORS remains a single fixed `FRONTEND_URL`; the server does not reflect arbitrary `Origin` values, and a probe from `https://evil.example` received no matching allow-origin header.

### Pass 1 continuation — denied standalone factory route (2026-10-04 UTC)

- Real API/UI reproduction as `ppc_head` showed `/factory` and `/factory/qc` correctly display the generic `Page not found` state because the actor lacks `production.operator.access`, but render no `<main>` landmark. The root cause is `PermissionGuard` running outside `FactoryFloorLayout`; the denied `NotFoundState` is a `<div>`, while the allowed touch shell supplies its own `<main>`. The focused regression failed with zero main landmarks on both denied routes and no console/API errors.
- Added an opt-in `standalone` mode to `PermissionGuard` and used it for the factory route. This wraps only its denied state in `<main>`; existing guards and the authorized factory shell retain their existing layout. The real regression passed **1/1**, and `ppc_head` route chunk 3/6 passed **1/1**. Targeted ESLint, full TypeScript check, `git diff --check`, and Impeccable detector passed.
- A separate `finance_officer` route chunk rerun passed **1/1**: `/accounting` rendered successfully when exercised alone. Its earlier blank record is attributed to the four-worker crawl under concurrent test load, not a reproduced product defect.
- The first four-worker role crawl stopped after **17 passed / 15 failed / 59 not run**. Confirmed failures include the factory denied-state landmark, now fixed. Dashboard skeleton/blank candidates had no recorded console or failed API request and remain unverified under isolated load; several factory routes also use different permission boundaries. This incomplete crawl is not a pass.

### Decision Question 27 — who may use Factory Floor PWA

Scenario: every seeded non-admin role lacks `production.operator.access`; only `system_admin` bypasses the frontend guard, so normal production staff cannot open the Factory Floor PWA.

Why unresolved: the permission is defined but never assigned by the role seeder, and no dedicated production-operator role or assignment workflow exists. The code describes an operator-scoped surface but does not identify which current role owns it.

Options: (1) grant it to `production_manager` (uses an existing role, but combines management and shop-floor actions); (2) add a least-privilege production-operator role and provision its users (clear separation, but requires role and onboarding decisions); (3) keep the surface admin-only until a role is approved (safe, but leaves operator screens unavailable).

Recommendation: option 2. Interim guard: non-admin users remain denied from this UI route; work-order API endpoints continue to require their own `production.work_orders.view`, `production.wo.confirm`, or `production.wo.record` permission. No role grants or schema are changed.

### Pass 1 continuation — sealed RFQ chain readbacks and classified diagnostics (2026-10-05 UTC)

- Real Chromium E2E `e2e-real/live-rfq-bidding.spec.ts` passed **1/1** in **1.6 minutes** on `http://localhost:18080` / `ogami_test_audit_20261002`. The chain used CRM to create a sales order, Purchasing to set and submit the auto-generated PR and create/publish/award the RFQ, Finance to approve the PR and PO, the supplier portal to submit a quote, the Vice President for the second PO approval, and Finance/QC for read-only comparison checks.
- Every RFQ gate was read back through the role's real API session before advancing: PR `pending` → Finance `approved`; RFQ `draft` → `open` → `closed` → `awarded`; supplier quote `submitted`; one RFQ award and one linked draft PO; PO `draft` → `pending_approval` with Finance approved and Vice President pending → `approved` with no pending approval record. Vice President could not start the RFQ (`can_start_rfq=false`); Purchasing could (`true`).
- The final `r7` test revision quotes all three requested lines at **₱600.00** each and independently recomputes the quote from API quantities. It blocks a four-decimal quantity inline before submit. Purchasing awards only the first **125**-unit line, producing a **₱75,000.00** PO subtotal, **₱9,000.00** VAT, and **₱84,000.00** total; API readbacks match through draft, pending approval, and approved states. Finance and QC can view the comparison but cannot award; the supplier sees the outcome.
- Direct precondition attacks before all invited suppliers responded returned **422** for early close (`every invited supplier has submitted`) and for comparison (`prices stay sealed`); the RFQ remained `open`. A duplicate award attempt is covered by `RfqBiddingTest` and returns **422** with no second PO.
- The first browser walk exposed stale test selectors (`No quote` checkbox, `Lead time days`, `Quotation comparison`, old award button labels); the live portal uses a default quoted line, `Can deliver by`, `Compare & award`, and `Award & create draft POs`. The chain test now matches observed UI and asserts API state/amounts after each step. Two intermediate failures were harness races: the test read approval state before the asynchronous approval request completed. Polling the persisted PO status fixed the check. Read-only verification of the previous run confirmed final PO status `approved`, both approval records approved, and `approved_at` set; no product defect was confirmed.
- Browser diagnostics captured **26** console errors and **26** HTTP **401** responses, all one-to-one `/api/v1/auth/user` checks while new contexts were unauthenticated. No uncaught page errors, failed requests, or other HTTP error responses occurred. The E2E now records and classifies this expected bootstrap response; API server logs during the run contained no 5xx or exception entries.
- **Decision Question 28 — quote timing information.** Scenario: supplier submits a quote without either `proposed_delivery_date` or `lead_time_days`, especially when the RFQ has no required date. Why unresolved: the server accepts both fields as optional; comparison displays `—` if both are absent; the supplier form exposes a calendar date but no lead-time field; repository rules do not say whether one is mandatory. Options: (1) require a delivery date; (2) add a lead-time field and require either date or lead time; (3) keep both optional and let Purchasing award with timing unknown. Recommendation: option 2 if buyer evaluation requires a timing commitment. Current behavior remains unchanged pending decision; no test in this pass submitted a timing-free quote.

Real Chromium created a new budget through Finance UI, asserted draft totals/audit, rejected draft approval with **422**, submitted as Finance, returned it to draft as VP with a reason, edited and resubmitted as Finance, then activated it as VP. After each action, the test read the persisted status, totals, actors/timestamps, audit trail, or pending transfer state before advancing. Duplicate budget approval and active-budget edit returned **422** with unchanged state.
- Real UI then requested a **₱10.00** January line transfer from the active budget. Pending state left both monthly allocations unchanged. Finance2, a different user holding the same Finance role, received a backend **422** and no mutation. VP approval moved exactly **₱10.00** between the same two lines; total budget remained unchanged; duplicate approval returned **422** with no second movement. Existing `BudgetTransferTest` also covers positive amounts, headroom, draft-budget refusal, cross-year/type refusal, closed fiscal year, rejection with no movement, and duplicate approval; transfer transition and role matrices remain incomplete.
- The real E2E first reproduced a visible, enabled **Approve** button for Finance2 even though the server rejected the role. Added a regression assertion that failed with **1** visible button. `BudgetTransferService::canApprove()` now owns the requester/current-approver role comparison used by both approval and the API eligibility field; list, detail, create, approve, and reject responses return server-derived `can_approve`. The Finance UI hides Approve unless both the local grant and server eligibility are true and explains that another role must approve. Direct API authorization remains authoritative. No schema change or migration.
- The Finance create page no longer blocks company-wide budget creation when its optional HR department lookup returns **403**. It permits company-wide creation, shows the lookup failure and retry affordance, and does not assign a department silently. A decision is still required on whether Finance may choose a department through a narrower budgeting-specific endpoint.
- Test-harness false failures were reproduced and corrected: login returned on `/dashboard` before the server-selected role dashboard had redirected, resetting a just-opened Finance menu; capture counted Vite `/src/api/...` modules as API requests; and a finance dashboard query was canceled by test navigation before its normal 200. The test now waits for the final dashboard route and initial API quiet before navigating. Capture tracks backend `/api/v1/` responses, while still recording HTTP errors for all requests. One Vite `recharts.js` **499** was an expected module cancellation during navigation; no browser E2E failure or API 5xx remained in the green run.
- Verification: the red role-control assertion failed before the UI fix (**1 button received, expected 0**). Targeted PHPUnit passed **5 tests / 41 assertions** on `ogami_test_suite_20261002`; Pint, ESLint, TypeScript, and `git diff --check` passed. Focused real-browser budget/transfer subset passed **3/3**, then the complete budgeting spec passed **7/7** in **1.5 minutes** on `http://localhost:18080` / `ogami_test_audit_20261002` using one worker. Exact full-spec command: `E2E_BASE_URL=http://localhost:18080 npx playwright test -c playwright.real.config.ts e2e-real/live-budgeting.spec.ts --project=real-chromium --reporter=line --output=/tmp/ogami-budgeting-final-green-20261005`. The manual app at `http://localhost:8081` and database `ogami` were not targeted.
- Browser diagnostics from the green run classified bootstrap `/auth/user` **401**, Finance's denied optional HR department lookup **403**, and asserted budget/transfer gate **422** responses. The Nginx test log also recorded one Vite module cancellation (`/node_modules/.vite/deps/recharts.js`, **499**) on navigation. No 5xx was present. One Laravel `OutboxEventCodec` `local.ERROR` for `MrpPlan#17 changed after publication` appeared at 04:57:37 in the app's `Asia/Manila` log timezone beside budget GETs that returned 200. The API file log is bind-mounted into both app stacks; the audit API logger defaults to `stderr`, while the manual API had an active queue worker when inspected. No matching current outbox row appeared across the isolated `ogami_test_*` databases queried. Source remains unverified and is not attributed to the audit run; reproduce it with isolated app logs before classifying it as a defect.
- **Decision Question 29 — Finance department selection.** Scenario: Finance creates or edits a budget with a department selected. Why unresolved: the current picker calls the HR department-tree endpoint, which Finance cannot view, while the rules and permissions do not establish whether Finance should get organizational department data. Options: (1) grant `hr.departments.view` to Finance (simple, broader HR visibility); (2) add a narrow budgeting department lookup protected by `budgeting.manage` (recommended, least privilege); (3) restrict Finance to company-wide budgets and route department assignments through another role (simple, limits Finance workflows). Interim guard: company-wide create remains available and the picker reports failure without silently selecting a department. Behavior remains undecided.
- Budget lifecycle coverage is still partial: budget draft/submit/return/resubmit/activate and active-edit refusal were exercised; closed/deleted/legacy-approved states and all forbidden transition cells were not. Transfer pending/approved and same-role/duplicate approval were exercised; rejected, canceled/deactivated-user, concurrent-approver, stale-edit, and all permission cells remain unverified. Full Budget/Transfer transition and permission matrices are open.
- Ten new transfer what-if angles for the next pass, all untested: requester role changes while pending; only eligible VP deactivated mid-chain; every eligible approver loses the grant; fiscal year closes between request and approval; source budget closes after request; source actuals sync changes available headroom before approval; source monthly allocation changes after request; two eligible VPs approve concurrently; stale tab retries after the other VP completes; and the destination line is deactivated or deleted while the transfer is pending.

### Pass 2 continuation — missing-currency fallback (2026-10-05 UTC)

- A new browser test returned **503** twice from the business-policy endpoint. It reproduced a live dashboard with totals rendered as bare **`1,250.00`**, with no currency label or symbol, after policy retries failed. Screenshot: `/tmp/ogami-portal-policy-failure-red-20261005/customer-portal-customer-p-ee872-ness-currency-settings-load-desktop-chromium/test-failed-1.png`.
- Root cause is the portal layout treating any fetched policy query, including terminal error, as currency-ready; clearing the missing currency causes `formatPeso` to emit an unqualified decimal. Expected safe behavior is to keep money pages blocked, show an actionable settings error, and allow retry. Regression is red; implementation and green verification pending after the active real RFQ browser suite, to avoid changing its mounted app during a workflow test.

### Pass 2 continuation — customer-return truck receipt and MRB release (2026-10-05 UTC)

The full delivery class passed after both fixes.

Inventory viewers and wildcard system admins receive it; outsiders and scrap dispositions do not. The regression verifies no alert while held, one alert after release, correct RMA and quantity text, and no duplicate alert after a repeated release is rejected. Notification suite passed **5 / 25**.

### Pass 2 continuation — delivery return precision and fresh verification (2026-10-05 UTC)

- A real-browser red reproduction proved the driver form rejected supported **0.001** truck-return and damage quantities: native inputs used `step=0.01`, and a **1.000** dispatch with **0.999** truck return displayed a **0.01** unaccounted residual instead of **0.001**. The backend accepts thousandths for physical truck quantities while customer-received ledger quantities remain limited to hundredths.
- `DeliveryAttemptPanel` now validates customer-ledger and physical quantities at their respective precisions, computes physical residuals as integer thousandths, preserves the submitted precision, and exposes `step=0.001` for return/damage inputs. The Chromium regression walks delivery creation, assignment, loading, transit, and driver outcome through the real UI; after each action it reads persisted state and stock effects before advancing. It passed **1/1** with exact `return_pending`, **0.999** return, **0.001** damage, and **0.001** unaccounted quantity; depot receipt remains the next required action. The test also checks the shared ImpEx view and captures browser/request diagnostics. No schema change or migration.

### Pass 2 continuation — planned work-order with zero-length window (2026-10-05 UTC)

- The real UI's default Planned start and Planned end are the same local minute. Against a fresh isolated fixture (`st109`), create produced a planned WO with equal persisted timestamps. Confirm opened the assignment review, which reported “No equipment slot available” and kept “Apply assignment and confirm” disabled. Readback showed no schedule, crew, equipment assignment, stock reservation, or status change. This is a guarded GAP, not yet a confirmed product defect; manual creation's existing frontend and backend rules allow equality, while the planning design does not settle whether same-time values represent a valid same-day plan.
- **Decision Question 30 — zero-length manual work-order window.** Scenario: PPC creates a manual work order with the default equal planned timestamps and cannot generate a capacity proposal. Why unresolved: current form/API allow equality, capacity planning needs time for a positive-duration job, and the detail UI has no planned-window edit path. Options: (1) require end after start and choose a standard default duration (clear rule, but chooses a scheduling policy); (2) add a PPC planning/edit step for planned records (recommended, preserves current date semantics but adds workflow); (3) treat these values as a date/due-date and have the planner choose an interval independently (keeps simple entry, changes planning semantics). Interim guard: review blocks assignment/confirmation with no writes; the user can cancel the planned record. The real Chromium regression now verifies the server rejection, unchanged state, and UI cancellation path.

The zero-length window stayed unassigned, `assignment-apply` rejected a nonexistent schedule with 422 and left status, crew, machine, mold, stock, inspections, notifications, and audit unchanged; PPC cancelled through the UI and the cancellation audit was recorded. Corrected probes now follow the current UI/API contract. No product defect confirmed.

- Manual app `http://localhost:8081` and database `ogami` remain untouched. Full pass and exit conditions remain open.
